and
W_{\widetilde{z}}
for different
z,\widetilde{z}\in Z_{N,R}
and obtain a lower bound for the entire interaction. Recall the notation from (
1.10
) and from (
2.18
)–(
2.20
). We extend (
2.19
) and (
2.20
) from
\varpi\in{\mathcal{S}}
to
\varpi_{W}=\sum_{g\in\Gamma}\delta_{g}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
, i.e., from
\Gamma\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}
to
\Gamma\subset{\mathcal{C}}_{W}
, in an obvious way. Then we estimate

\Phi_{\Lambda_{N},\Lambda_{N}}(\omega)\geq\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}F_{W,W}(\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}),\theta_{z}(\varpi_{W_{z}})),

(4.28)

where
F_{W,W}(\omega_{W},\varpi_{W})=\frac{1}{2}F_{W,W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega_{W})+F_{W,W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega_{W},\varpi_{W})+\frac{1}{2}F_{W,W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\varpi_{W})
.

This implies the following for all sufficiently large
N
:

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})

\displaystyle\leq{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},\rm bc}})}}}\Big[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\frac{1}{|W|}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}_{2\delta}(\rho_{1})\Big\}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\times{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\frac{1}{|W|}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}_{2\delta}(\rho_{2})\Big\}\prod_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{\operatorname{e}}^{-F_{W,W}(\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}),\theta_{z}(\varpi_{W_{z}}))}\Big].

We now write the right-hand side of (
4.29
) in terms of the empirical measure
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P}}})}}}
from (
4.20
) and its boundary-shred projection
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
from (
4.23
) as follows. Recall (
4.16
) for writing the particle number in shreds as a functional of
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
. From this, the assertion follows.
∎

For later developments in Section
6
, when we will take the limit as
N\to\infty
, it will be convenient to work exclusively with particle boundary condition, which was introduced below (
1.4
). Therefore, let us further estimate from above, until we arrive at a similar expectation with
{\rm bc}={\rm par}
. For ‘bc’ being Dirichlet zero boundary conditions, this is trivial, since zero boundary conditions in particular satisfy particle boundary conditions. For periodic boundary conditions, however, we have to work harder for this point; this will be done in Sections
5
and at the beginning of Section
6.1
.

At the beginning of Section
6.1
we will make some technical preparations for applying a suitable large-deviation principle in the limit as
N\to\infty
to the right-hand side of (
4.25
). A suitable formulation of this LDP will be in Corollary
6.5
. After inserting in Section
6.3
some events that induce some necessary compactness and control on the particle number and interaction, we will apply the LDP in Section
6.4
, resulting in an
R
- and
\delta
-depending variational formula. This will be used in Section
9.1
to make
R\to\infty
and
\delta\downarrow 0
, and to finish the proof of the upper bound in Theorem
2.5
.

4.6.
Lower bound

We are going to derive a lower bound for
\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
defined in (
4.24
) for any sufficiently large
N
. The main result of this section is Lemma
4.3
below. Our lower bound there will be analogous to the upper bound in Lemma
4.2
, but will require much more restrictions in the integration area.

In general, we assume the situation and notation described in Section
4.4
. We will have to neglect all the interactions between loops and shreds in different subboxes
W_{z}
, such that the contributions will be independent over the subboxes. For this, we need to restrict the amount of particles in the boundary area of
W_{z}
for any
z\in Z_{N,R}
. We will do that in a much more comprehensive way than what is needed here, in order to prepare for future steps in Section
6.5
, where we will take the limit as
N\to\infty
. That is, we will impose here that, in each subbox
W_{z}
, all the legs (of loops and of shreds) have a
spread

\|f\|_{\rm sp}=\max_{t\in[0,\beta]}|f(t)-f(0)|
that is
<M
, and in each ball of radius
\mathfrak{r}
(to be introduced), the particle number is
\leq T
for some large
M,T
. Furthermore, we need to restrict the global configuration close to the boundary of
\Lambda_{N}
in order to achieve particle boundary condition.

We are going to use
C\in(0,\infty)
as a generic constant that depends only on
d
,
\beta
or
v
(which we neglect) and may change its value from appearance to appearance. If it may depend on additional parameters, then these are appended as indices.

We need to restrict to configurations in

{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}=\Big\{\sum_{x\in\zeta}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}\in{\mathcal{L}}\colon\|f_{x,i}\|_{\rm sp}<M\ \forall x\in\zeta,\forall i\Big\},\qquad M\in(1,\infty),

(4.30)

where we recall that
f_{i}
is the
i
-th leg of the loop
f
. We will write
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}={\mathcal{L}}_{W}\cap{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
. We will make a change of measure from the original PPP
{\tt Q}
(see Definition
1.1
) to the PPP
{\tt Q}_{M}
with intensity measure

\nu_{\Lambda_{N},{M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm par}})}}}({\rm d}x,{\rm d}f)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{k}{\rm d}x\otimes\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,{M},\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}({\rm d}f),

(4.31)

where
\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,{M},\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}
is the restriction of
\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}
to the set of loops of length
k
whose legs have spread
<M
. The
W_{R}
-shred-configurations under
{\tt Q}_{M}
lie in the set

{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}=\Big\{\sum_{g\in\Gamma}\delta_{g}\in{\mathcal{S}}\colon\|g_{i}\|_{\rm sp}<M\ \forall g\in\Gamma,i\in\mathbb{Z}\Big\}.

(4.32)

We also need a notation for the number of all particles in
W\Subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, regardless where their loop or shred comes from:

\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{W}(\omega,\varpi)=\sum_{x\in\zeta}\sum_{i}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{f_{x,i}(0)\in W\}+\sum_{g\in\Gamma}\sum_{i}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{g_{i}(0)\in W\},\qquad\omega\in{\mathcal{L}},\varpi\in{\mathcal{S}}.

(4.33)

With a parameter
\mathfrak{r}\in(0,1)
, we decompose (up to the boundaries)
W
into the boxes
z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
with
z\in W\cap 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. With positive parameters
L,K,T
, define the events

\displaystyle A_{W;M,L,K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigcap_{z\in W\cap 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big\{\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\leq L}})}}}\colon\forall l\in[L]\colon|\zeta_{z,l}|\leq K\Big\},

(4.34)

\displaystyle A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigcap_{z\in W\cap 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big\{\varpi\in{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\colon\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\varpi)\leq T\Big\},

(4.35)

where
\zeta_{z,l}
is the set of initial points of
l
-length loops of
\omega
in
z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
. That is, the loop configurations in
A_{W;M,L,K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
have only legs with spread
<M
, only loop lengths
\leq L
, and there are, in any of the subboxes
z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
, no more
l
-length loops than
K
, while in
A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
we impose a uniform upper bound for the number of particles in any of the subboxes
z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
.

We will later restrict the integration with respect to the PPP
\omega_{\rm P}
to the event

A_{W;\Theta}=A_{W;M,L,K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\times A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}},\qquad\mbox{with }\Theta=(M,L,K,\mathfrak{r},T).

(4.36)

More precisely, we will restrict to the event
\bigcap_{z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}}\{(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})\in A_{W_{z};\Theta}\}
, where we introduce the slightly smaller (than
\Lambda_{N}
) box
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}=\bigcup_{z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}}W_{z}
, and
Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
is the set of all
z\in Z_{N,R}
that have no neighbour in
Z_{N,R}^{\rm c}
(where we adopt the usual neighbourhood relation in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, upscaled by a factor of
2R
). Let us introduce the conditional version of
{{\tt Q}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\rm par}})}}}[\,\cdot\,|\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi]
given this event, i.e., given that
(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})\in A_{W_{z};\Theta}
for all
z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
, which we denote by
{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}[\,\cdot\,|\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi]
. The slightly modified empirical measure is denoted

\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega}})}}}=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}^{\circ}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}}\delta_{\big(\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}),\theta_{z}(\varpi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}})\big)}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})

(4.37)

and
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
is the boundary-shred projection of
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega}})}}}
analogously to (
4.23
).
We also need the function

p_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}(\mu)={\tt K}_{W}\big(\mu;A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\big),\qquad\mu\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W}.

(4.38)

As usual, we write
W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}
for short.

Lemma 4.3

(Lower bound)
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in[0,\infty)
. Then, for any
M,L,K,T,\mathfrak{r}\in(0,\infty)
, there are constants
c_{M}\in(0,1)
satisfying
\lim_{M\to\infty}c_{M}=1
and
C_{M,L,K,T}\in(0,\infty)
(depending only on
M
respectively only on
M,L,K,T
) such that, for any
N,R\in\mathbb{N}
such that
R>M
, and for any
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
(possibly depending on
N
) such that the following conditional expectation is well-defined,

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}

\displaystyle(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{-\frac{1}{R}{C_{M,L,K,T}}|\Lambda_{N}|}{\operatorname{e}}^{-o(|\Lambda_{N}|)}c_{M}^{|\Lambda_{N}|}\big[\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})(A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W;M,L,K})\big]^{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|/|W|}{\operatorname{e}}^{\frac{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}{|W|}\langle\log p_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T},\psi\rangle}

(4.39)

\displaystyle\times{{\tt Q}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\rm par}})}}}\big(\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi\big)

\displaystyle\times{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}\Big[\exp\Big\{-|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|\big\langle\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega_{\rm P}}})}}},\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}F_{W,W}\big\rangle\Big\}

\displaystyle\times{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\langle\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega_{\rm P}}})}}},\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{1})\Big\}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\langle\psi,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{\partial W}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{2})\Big\}\Big|\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi\Big].

Proof.

In the expectation in (
4.24
) that defines
\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
we insert the indicator on the event that the configuration lies in
{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
. Furthermore, we now require that the configuration
\omega_{\Lambda_{N}}
has no particle in the boundary area
\Lambda_{N}\setminus\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
. This implies that the restricted configuration
\omega_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}
satisfies particle-boundary conditions in
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
(which was introduced below (
1.4
)), since no loop reaches
\partial\Lambda_{N}
(because of
M<R
) and this holds for both, Dirichlet zero and periodic boundary condition in
\Lambda_{N}
. The event of having no particles in
\Lambda_{N}\setminus\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
is the intersection of the two independent events that no loops start in
\Lambda_{N}\setminus\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
(whose probability is
\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{-o(|\Lambda_{N}|)}
, since the volume of
\Lambda_{N}\setminus\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
is
o(|\Lambda_{N}|)
) and the event that no shred terminates in
\Lambda_{N}\setminus\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
, which is absorbed in the expectation with respect to particle-boundary conditions in
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
. Hence, we will restrict to an expectation on configurations in
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
instead of
\Lambda_{N}
, which will be asymptotically lead to no changes in the limit as
N\to\infty
.

Note that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\big([\nu_{\Lambda_{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm par}})}}}-\nu_{\Lambda_{N},{M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm par}})}}}](\Lambda_{N}\times{\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}})\big)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{k}\mu_{0,0}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}}_{k}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}}_{k,M})\to 0\qquad\mbox{as }M\to\infty,

where
{\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}}_{k,M}
denotes the set of loops of length
k
whose legs have spread
<M
. Furthermore, the number of loops in the event in (
4.24
) is
\leq O(|\Lambda_{N}|)
; hence the change of measure from
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}
to
{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}
yields, as a lower bound a factor of
c_{M}^{|\Lambda_{N}|}
with some
c_{M}\in(0,1)
satisfying
c_{M}\to 1
as
M\to\infty
.

Summarizing, this transition to the
M
-restricted configurations in
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
instead of
\Lambda_{N}
leads to the bound

\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{o(|\Lambda_{N}|)}c_{M}^{|\Lambda_{N}|}{{\tt Q}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\rm par}})}}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|\textstyle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,\neg R}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}-\rho_{1}|\leq\textstyle{\frac{\delta}{2}}\}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|\textstyle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,R}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}-\rho_{2}|\leq\textstyle{\frac{\delta}{2}}\}\Big].

(4.40)

Our next step is to restrict to the event
\{\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi\}
for some (later particularly chosen)
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
, where
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega}})}}}
is defined in (
4.37
).

Here we will pick
\psi
(possibly depending on
N
) such that this event has positive probability, and hence we write (
4.40
) as

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}

\displaystyle{}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{o(|\Lambda_{N}|)}c_{M}^{|\Lambda_{N}|}{{\tt Q}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\rm par}})}}}\big(\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi\big)

(4.41)

\displaystyle\times{{\tt Q}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\rm par}})}}}\Big[{\rm e}^{-\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|\textstyle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,\neg R}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}-\rho_{1}|<\textstyle{\frac{\delta}{2}}\}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|\textstyle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,R}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}-\rho_{2}|<\textstyle{\frac{\delta}{2}}\}\,\Big|\,\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi\Big].

Our next task is to write the expectation in the second line of (
4.41
) as an expectation with respect to
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega_{\rm P}}})}}}
, which is not able to express interaction between any two subboxes
z+W
and
z^{\prime}+W
with distinct
z.z^{\prime}\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. For this, we need to split all the interaction into the sum of the interactions in all the subboxes
z+W_{R}
with
z\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and we need to upper estimate the interaction between any two of such subboxes. This will be based on the following estimate of the interaction in terms of an integral of the square of particle numbers. Recall the notation from (
1.10
) and from (
2.18
)–(
2.20
) and (
4.26
) and that
F_{W,W}=\frac{1}{2}F_{W,W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}+F_{W,W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}+\frac{1}{2}F_{W,W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
. For two (not necessarily disjoint) subboxes
W,\widetilde{W}
, we write
F_{W,\widetilde{W}}((\omega_{W},\varpi_{W}),(\omega_{\widetilde{W}},\varpi_{\widetilde{W}}))
for all the interaction of loops and shreds between
W
and
\widetilde{W}
(i.e., the loops with all their particles in
W
respectively in
\widetilde{W}
).

Lemma 4.4

(Estimating interaction)
.

Assume that the interaction potential
v
satisfies Assumption (V). Then, for any
M\in(0,\infty)
, with
\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}=M+\sup{\operatorname{supp}}(v)
, for any configuration
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
, and for any
R\in(1,\infty)
and any
z,z^{\prime}\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\displaystyle F_{W_{z},W_{z^{\prime}}}\big((\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}}),(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|v\|_{\infty}\Big(\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

(4.42)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})^{2}\Big)^{1/2},

where
{\mathcal{B}}_{r}(A)
denotes the
r
-ball around a compact set
A
.

Proof.

We write
\mathfrak{r}
instead of
\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}
. The main observation is that any leg of
\omega
in
W
(i.e., any leg that starts from a particle in
W
) stays in the
M
-neighbourhood of
W
and has therefore interaction only in the
\mathfrak{r}
-neighbourhood of
W
. Hence, any
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
has the property that, for any two legs
g,g^{\prime}
of a loop or shred in
W_{z}
and of a loop or shred in
W_{z^{\prime}}
(meaning that the initial site lies in
W_{z}
respectively in
W_{z^{\prime}}
), respectively, that have a non-vanishing interaction with each other, there is a site
x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})
such that
|g(0)-x|\leq\mathfrak{r}
and
|g^{\prime}(0)-x|\leq\mathfrak{r}
. Denote by
\mathfrak{L}(\omega_{W},\varpi_{W})=\{f_{x,i}\colon x\in\zeta\cap W,i\in[\ell(f_{x})]\}\cup\{g_{i}\colon i\in\Gamma_{W}(g),g\in\varpi_{W}\}
the set of all the legs of loops or of shreds of
\omega_{W}=\sum_{x\in\zeta}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}
or of
\varpi_{W}
. (Recall that all particles of all the loops of
\omega_{W}
are in
W
, according to (
4.26
).) Then, for any
z,z^{\prime}\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\displaystyle F_{W_{z},W_{z^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\big((\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}}),(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{g\in\mathfrak{L}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})}\sum_{g^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{L}(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})}V(g,g^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|v\|_{\infty}\sum_{g,g^{\prime}}\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x\,{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|g(0)-x|\leq\mathfrak{r}\}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|g^{\prime}(0)-x|\leq\mathfrak{r}\}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|v\|_{\infty}\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|v\|_{\infty}\Big(\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\int_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W_{z}\cap W_{z^{\prime}})}{\rm d}x\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

∎

In words, this estimates the amount of cross-interaction between two neighbouring subboxes against the product of the integrals over
x
in a neighbourhood of the intersection of the subboxes over the square of particle numbers in a certain box around
x
, and the amount of interaction within one of such subboxes in terms of the integral of the squares of the particle numbers in a neighbourhood of the subbox.

Let us proceed with the proof of Lemma
4.3
, restarting from (
4.41
) and showing what Lemma
4.4
gives us for the interaction.
We will, as usual, always write
\omega=\sum_{x\in\zeta}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}
for any
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}
. We write
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\leq L}})}}}=\{\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\colon\ell(f_{x})\leq L\,\forall x\in\zeta\}
. We pick now an arbitrary
\mathfrak{r}\in(0,\infty)
. Without loss of generality, we assume that the boxes
z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
with
z\in W\cap 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
cover
W
, i.e.,
W=\bigcup_{z\in W\cap 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
. Recall from Remark
2.11
that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{V}
counts particles only within
V
for any
V\subset W
; hence for
(\omega,\varpi)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
, we have that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega,\varpi)=\sum_{z\in W\cap 2\mathfrak{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}}(\omega,\varpi)
.

For
\omega\in A_{W;M,L,K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
we easily see that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega)\leq C_{M,L,K}
for every
x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W)
with some
C_{M,L,K}
that depends only on
M,L,
and
K
. Analogously, for
\varpi\in A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
we easily see that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v}})}}}}(x)}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\varpi)\leq C_{M}T
for every
x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(W)
with some
C_{M}\in(0,\infty)
that depends only on
M
.

On the event
A_{W;\Theta}
defined in (
4.36
), since
R
is much larger than
M
, for each
z\in Z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}
, the number of
z^{\prime}\not=z
such that there is some interaction between
W_{z}
and
W_{z^{\prime}}
is not larger than
3^{d}
. Furthermore, the volume of
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{r}}(\partial W_{z})
is not larger than
CMR^{d-1}
. Hence, we can estimate, with the help of Lemma
4.4
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}(\omega)

\displaystyle=\sum_{z,z^{\prime}\in Z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}}F_{W_{z},W_{z^{\prime}}}\big((\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}}),(\omega_{W_{z^{\prime}}},\varpi_{W_{z^{\prime}}})\big)

(4.43)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in Z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}}\Big(F_{W_{z},W_{z}}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})+C_{M,L,K,T}R^{d-1}\Big)\leq\frac{|\Lambda_{N}|}{|W|}\langle F_{W,W},\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega}})}}}\rangle+\frac{C_{M,L,K,T}}{R}|\Lambda_{N}|,

with some
C_{M,L,K,T}\in(0,\infty)
that does not depend on
R
nor on
\omega
nor on
N
. (We choose the event
A_{W;\Theta}
much smaller than what is needed for (
4.43
); this will come in handy in our further developments in Section
6.5
.)

So far, we have argued that we may estimate, for any sufficiently large
N
,

\displaystyle{\operatorname{e}}^{-\Phi_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|\textstyle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,\neg R}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}-\rho_{1}|\leq\textstyle{\frac{\delta}{2}}\}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{|\textstyle{\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,R}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}}-\rho_{2}|\leq\textstyle{\frac{\delta}{2}}\}

(4.44)

\displaystyle\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{-|\Lambda_{N}|\frac{1}{|W|}\langle F_{W,W},\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}\rangle}{\operatorname{e}}^{-\frac{C_{M,L,K,T}}{R}|\Lambda_{N}|}\prod_{z\in Z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{A_{W_{z};\Theta}}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})

\displaystyle\times{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}}\frac{1}{|W|}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/2}(\rho_{1})\Big\}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}}\frac{1}{|W|}{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/2}(\rho_{2})\Big\}.

Under
{{\tt Q}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},\rm par}})}}}[\,\cdot\,|\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi]
, the indicators
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{A_{W_{z};\Theta}}(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})
are independent over
z\in Z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}_{N,R}
and their product has the probability

\big[\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})(A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W;M,L,K})\big]^{|Z_{N,R}^{\circ}|}{\operatorname{e}}^{|Z_{N,R}^{\circ}|\langle\log p_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T},\psi\rangle},

where
p_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}
is defined in (
4.38
).
Using the notation for the conditional measure
{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N}^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}[\,\cdot\,|\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi]
given just before the lemma, we obtain the assertion of the lemma, by substituting (
4.44
) into (
4.41
).
∎

This lower bound is analogous to the upper bound in (
4.25
) with
Z_{N,R}
replaced by
Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
and the corresponding amendments and with
\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}_{2\delta}(\rho_{i})
replaced by
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{i})
and with additional auxiliary terms whose exponential rates turn out to be small when we later make
R,M,T,L\to\infty
. Note that we have now particle boundary condition in the (slightly smaller) box
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
. Therefore, all the
\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}})
with
z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
have now the same distribution; in particular the
\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}})
are i.i.d.

In Lemma
5.3
below, we are going to take
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
in a particular way and derive a suitable large-deviation lower bound for the term in the second line in Section
5.2
. In Corollary
6.5
in Section
6.1
we explain abstractly how to find the large-
N
exponential rate of the expectation in the last two lines, and in Section
6.5
we proceed with making
N\to\infty
in (
4.39
). In Section
9.2
we put everything together and finish the proof of the lower bound in Theorem
2.5
.

5.
The empirical measure of boundary-shred configurations

In this section we provide crucial information about the distribution of the empirical measure of the boundary-shred configuration,
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
, defined in (
4.23
), for the reference marked PPP
\omega_{\rm P}
under
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
. The main result of this section will be a lower bound for the large-
N
deviations in Lemma
5.3
. This will be crucial for handling the term in the second line of (
4.39
). Finally, this term should and will turn out to be negligible in the limit
R\to\infty
since it is “only” a boundary term after all, but the way to proving this will be long. A step towards this is done in Lemma
5.5
, where the lower bound of Lemma
5.3
is further lower bounded for particular configurations. More work in Section
7.5
will show that a general configuration can be approximated with such particular ones. In contrast, for proving the upper bound, we will just drop the influence coming from
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
, i.e., we estimate probabilities involving this just by one, which is of course much easier.

In Lemma
5.3
and its proof we make a connection between long loops and interlacements; we need to construct large-loop configurations with a given empirical measure
\psi
of boundary-shred configurations. By far not every
\psi
admits the existence of loop-configurations in arbitrarily large boxes
\Lambda_{N}
that is consistent with
\psi
, the criterion for this property is rather subtle and has a global character. In Lemma
5.3
we first circumvent this problem by coining the term “admissible” for
\psi
’s having this extension property and prove the lemma under this assumption. But in Lemma
5.4
we show that the requirement of ergodicity of shift-invariant random interlacement configurations implies admissibility; this is the reason that in Section
7.5
we need to approximate arbitrary loop-interlacement configurations with ergodic ones.

As a preparation, in Section
5.1
we look at the
W
-shreds emanating from a single loop and describe, given the boundary-shred configuration, the distribution of the resulting shred configuration via the strong Markov property of the Brownian motion. This is used in Section
5.2
in Lemma
5.3
for deriving the crucial LDP lower bound for the probability of the event
\{\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\approx\psi\}
for any admissible
\psi
. In Section
5.3
we derive further estimates for this lower bound for particular shred distributions. Furthermore, as an application and also for independent interest and further use later, we show that the expected number of shreds in a large box is negligible with respect to the volume of the box for any shift-invariant interlacement configuration measure with bounded entropy and bounded expected particle density; a property that is very suggesting, but surprisingly difficult to prove.

In the entire section, assume that the situation and the notation described in Section
1.4
is given. That is, the box
\Lambda_{N}
with volume
\sim N/\rho
is decomposed into
N_{R}\sim|\Lambda_{N}|/|W_{R}|
regular boxes
W_{z}=z+W
of diameter
2R
, where
R
may depend on
N
and converges to a given
R\in\mathbb{N}
.

5.1.
The shreds of one loop

In this section, we are examining the joint distribution of all the
W
-shreds of a given Brownian loop in a large box
\Lambda
containing the box
W
. Using the strong Markov property, we will collect all the pieces between entries and exits to and from
W
and drop the remaining pieces. That is, we will consider one
W
-shred
g\in{\mathcal{C}}_{k}
as in (
4.8
), ignoring the last particle before
g(0)
, which is in
W^{\rm c}
. Recall that
\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}
denotes the distribution of a Brownian motion
B=(B_{t})_{t\in[0,\infty)}
in
\Lambda
with boundary condition ‘bc’  starting from
x
. We assume that ‘bc’ is one of the three boundary conditions ‘per’, ‘0’ or ‘par’.

Define

p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(l,y)=\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(\tau=l,B_{\tau\beta}\in{\rm d}y)/{\rm d}y,\qquad(x,l,y)\in\Lambda\times\mathbb{N}\times\Lambda,

(5.1)

where

\tau=\tau_{W}=\inf\{l\in\mathbb{N}\colon B_{l\beta}\notin W^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B_{0}}})}}}\}

(5.2)

is the first of the times in
\beta\mathbb{N}
at which the motion changes the box, where we write
W^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}=W_{z_{x}}
with
x\in W_{z_{x}}
for that subbox that contains
x
. Then
p_{x}
is a sub-probability density (with respect to the counting measure on
\mathbb{N}
times the Lebesgue measure) on
\mathbb{N}\times(W^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}})^{\rm c}
(for periodic boundary condition even a probability density). Furthermore, for a loop
B\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}}
, we define
\tau_{1}=\tau
and
\tau_{j+1}=\inf\{l>\tau_{j}\colon l\in\mathbb{N},B_{l\beta}\notin W^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B_{\tau_{j}}}})}}}\}
(the
(j+1)
-st time that the motion
B
changes the box at some time in
\beta\mathbb{Z}
) for
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Since
B
is a cycle, there is a unique smallest
\mathfrak{m}_{R}=\mathfrak{m}_{R}(B)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
B_{\tau_{\mathfrak{m}_{R}+1}\beta}=B_{\tau_{1}\beta}
. The number
\mathfrak{m}_{R}(B)
is the number of times that
B
changes the subbox at times in
\beta\mathbb{N}
.

In order to describe the pieces of the loop between the changes between the subboxes (i.e., the shreds), we introduce the following probability measure on
{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
: by

q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l,W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}_{x,y}({\rm d}f)=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(\tau=l,B\in{\rm d}f,B_{\tau\beta}\in{\rm d}y)}{p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(l,y)\,{\rm d}y}=\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}\big((B_{s})_{s\in[0,l\beta]}\in{\rm d}f\mid\tau=l,B_{l\beta}=y\big)

(5.3)

we denote the distribution of a Brownian
W
-shred with
l
legs from
x\in W
to
y\in W^{\rm c}
.

We also need the restricted versions of
p_{x}
and
q_{x,y}
for shreds whose legs have spreads
<M
for some
M\in(0,\infty)
. For this, we denote

{\mathcal{C}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}=\{f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}\colon\|f\|_{\rm sp}<M\},\qquad\mbox{where }\|f\|_{\rm sp}=\max_{t\in[0,\beta]}|f(t)-f(0)|.

(5.4)

Denote

p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l,y)=\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(\tau=l,\forall j\in[l]\colon B_{j}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}},B_{\tau\beta}\in{\rm d}y\big)/{\rm d}y,\qquad(x,l,y)\in W\times\mathbb{N}\times W^{\rm c},

(5.5)

and

\displaystyle q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l,W,M}})}}}_{x,y}({\rm d}f)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{x}(\tau=l,\forall j\in[l]\colon B_{j}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}},B\in{\rm d}f,B(\tau\beta)\in{\rm d}y)}{p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l,y)\,{\rm d}y}

(5.6)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{x}\big(B\in{\rm d}f\mid\tau=l,B(l\beta)=y,\forall j\in[l]\colon B_{j}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\big).

Note that
B
does not leave the
M
-neighbourhood of the subbox in which it started. Hence, if this box is in the interior of
\Lambda
, then it satisfies particle-boundary condition in
\Lambda
.

We are going to identify the distribution of the lengths of the time lags between subsequent exit times from subboxes and the location of the Brownian cycle at these times.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
we consider the (non-normalised) measure

\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(\cdot)=\int_{\Lambda}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(\{\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1\}\cap\cdot).

(5.7)

Recall the decomposition of
\Lambda_{N}
into the subboxes
z+W
with
z\in Z_{N,R}
and the smaller box
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
, which is the union of the
z+W
over
z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
, the (discrete) interior of
Z_{N,R}
, as introduced before Lemma
4.3
.

Lemma 5.1

(Distribution of boundary shred configuration of one loop)
.

Fix
R\in(0,\infty)
and
W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}
and
M\in(0,R)
. Assume that
\Lambda
is a large centred box.

(1)

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
l_{1},l_{2},\dots,l_{m}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}l_{j}=k
. Fix
w_{1},\dots,w_{m}\in W
and
z_{1},\dots,z_{m}\in Z_{N,R}
such that
z_{j}\not=z_{j-1}
for any
j
, where
z_{0}=z_{m}
. Then

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}

\displaystyle\Big(\mathfrak{m}(B)=m,\forall j=1,\dots,m\colon\tau_{j}-\tau_{j-1}=l_{j},B_{l_{j}\beta}\in{\rm d}(z_{j}+w_{j})\Big)

(5.8)

\displaystyle=\bigotimes_{j=1}^{m}p_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}(l_{i},z_{j}+w_{j})\,{\rm d}w_{j},\qquad z_{0}=z_{m},w_{0}=w_{m}.

Furthermore, under conditioning on the event

A_{m}\big((l_{j},w_{j})_{j\in[m]}\big)=\{\mathfrak{m}_{R}(B)=m,\forall j=1,\dots,m\colon\tau_{j+1}-\tau_{j}=l_{j},B_{l_{j}\beta}=z_{j}+w_{j}\},

(5.9)

the shreds
(B_{s+\tau_{j}\beta})_{s\in[0,l_{j}\beta]}
are independent over
j\in\{1,\dots,m\}
and have distribution
q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{j},W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1},z_{j}+w_{j}}
.

(2)

For
{\rm bc}={\rm par}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}}
restricted to all the legs of
B
lying in
{\mathcal{C}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
, we can replace
p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}}
by
p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}
and
q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{j},W,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}
by
q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{j},W,M}})}}}
in these two statements.

(3)

For any
z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
such that all legs start start in
W_{z}
have spread
<M
and for any
j
satisfying
z_{j-1}=z
, we may replace
p_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm per}}})}}}
by
p_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}
and
q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{j},W,\Lambda,{\rm per}}})}}_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1},z_{j}+w_{j}}
by
q^{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{j},W,M}})}}_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1},z_{j}+w_{j}}
in these statements.

Proof.

(1) For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\widetilde{l}_{1},l_{2},\dots,l_{m}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\widetilde{l}_{1}+\sum_{j=2}^{m-1}l_{j}\leq k
, and
z_{1},\dots,z_{m}\in Z_{N,R}
satisfying
z_{j}\not=z_{j-1}
for all
j
and
w_{1},\dots,w_{m}\in W
, we have, with
\tau_{0}=0
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}\big(

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\widetilde{l}_{1},B_{\widetilde{l}_{1}\beta}\in{\rm d}(z_{1}+w_{1}),\forall j=1,\dots,m-1\colon\tau_{j}-\tau_{j-1}=l_{j},B_{l_{j}\beta}\in{\rm d}\big(z_{j}+w_{j}\big)\big)

(5.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda}\,{\rm d}x_{0}\,p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}_{x_{0}}\big(\widetilde{l}_{1},{\rm d}(z_{1}+w_{1})\big)\Big[\prod_{j=2}^{m}p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1}}\big(l_{j},{\rm d}(z_{j}+w_{j})\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}_{z_{m}+w_{m}}\Big(\tau>k-\widetilde{l}_{1}-\sum_{j=2}^{m}l_{j},B_{(k-\widetilde{l}_{1}-\sum_{j=2}^{m}l_{j})\beta}\in{\rm d}x_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{m}p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda,{\rm bc}}})}}}_{z_{j-1}+w_{j-1}}\big(l_{j},z_{j}+w_{j}\big)\,{\rm d}w_{j},\qquad l_{1}=k-\sum_{j=2}^{m}l_{j},z_{0}=z_{m},w_{0}=w_{m},

where we used the Markov property in the second step to concatenate the last piece of the Brownian cycle from the last change of a subbox at time
\tau_{m}\beta
until the end of the time interval,
k\beta
.

The additional assertion stems also directly from the strong Markov property, applied successively at the times
\tau_{j}\beta
.

(2) follows from the definition of
p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}
, and (3) from the fact that, by
M<R
, the legs in such subboxes to not reach the boundary of
\Lambda
but their particles stay in the subbox, and hence periodic boundary condition in
\Lambda
can be replaced by particle boundary condition in
W
.
∎

5.2.
Large-deviation lower bound for the boundary-shred configurations

In this section we show how to lower bound the term in the second line on the right-hand side of (
4.39
). That is, we now use Lemma
5.1
to derive a large-deviations lower bound for the distribution of the boundary configuration
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
defined in (
4.23
) of all the
W
-shreds, i.e., their lengths and initial and starting sites, for shreds coming from the
R
-crossing part of marked Poisson process
\omega_{\rm P}
.

The distribution of
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
is not easy to describe. As it concerns large-deviation upper bounds, we will in Section
9.1
be satisfied with estimating the probability of
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
being in a compact set simply against one. However, for the lower bound we have to work. Because of our preparations in Lemma
4.3
, we need the lower bound only for particle boundary conditions. We will be consent with a large-deviation lower bound for the probability of the event
\{\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi\}
for some given
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
(possibly
N
-dependent) with certain properties. For a general
\psi
, it is not
a priori
clear whether or not this event has positive probability at all under any loop configuration measure in a large box
\Lambda_{N}
, i.e., whether or not it is possible to find a loop configuration in
\Lambda_{N}
such that the empirical measure of its boundary-shred triples is close to the given
\psi
. For the time being we are therefore working under the following assumption on
\psi
.

Definition 5.2

(Admissible boundary-shred configurations)
.

Fix
R>0
and
W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}
. We call
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
admissible (more precisely,
W
-admissible) if, for any open neighbourhood
{\mathcal{B}}
of
\psi
, for any sufficiently large
N
, there is a loop-configuration
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\Lambda_{N}}
such that
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}
.

Actually, we will later need to apply the lower bound only to
\psi
’s of the form
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
with
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
. An important ingredient will then be Lemma
5.4
, which says that such a
\psi
is
W
-admissible if
P
is
W
-ergodic.

Recall the particle-number operator
\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
from (
4.16
) and observe that the expected shred particle number of a configuration
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
may be written, if
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
,

\langle{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W},\xi\rangle=\langle{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{\partial W},\psi\rangle=\int\psi({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i\in I}l_{i},\qquad\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}.

By
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x)
we always denote the open
\delta
-ball around
x
for any
x
in a metric space. If
x=\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
, then we use the Prokhorov metric defined in (
4.5
), which induces the weak topology.

The following lemma will later be applied for
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
and
Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
instead of
\Lambda_{N}
and
Z_{N,R}
. Recall that
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}
is the PPP with intensity measure given in (
4.31
), i.e., with all leg spreads
<M
. Analogously,
\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M}})}}}
is the canonical Brownian loop measure starting at
x
with
k
particles and all leg spreads
<M
. We recall from Section
5.1
that
\mathfrak{m}_{R}(B)
is the number of times that the loop
B
changes the subbox of radius
R
. By
C_{M}
we denote some constant
\in(0,\infty)
that depends only on
M
( and possibly on
d
and
\beta
).

Lemma 5.3

(Lower LDP bound for boundary shred configurations)
.

Assume the situation and the notation described in Section
1.4
. That is,
\Lambda_{N}
is a centred box with volume
|\Lambda_{N}|\sim N/\rho
for some
\rho\in(0,\infty)
, and is decomposed into the subboxes
z+W_{R}=z+[-R,R]^{d}
for some
R\in(0,\infty)
with
z\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap\Lambda_{N}
. We consider particle boundary condition and indicate it by ‘par’. Fix a large parameter
M\in(0,R/2)
and fix
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
such that
\langle{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{\partial W},\psi\rangle<\infty
, where
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
, and assume that
\psi
is
W
-admissible. Then, for any sufficiently large
R
, there is
\psi_{N}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
that converges to
\psi
as
N\to\infty
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}

\displaystyle\log{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}\Big(\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}=\psi_{N}\Big)\geq-\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}}+\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle,

(5.11)

where we recall (
5.5
) and introduce

\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}(\mu)=\sum_{i\in I}\log p_{x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l_{i},y_{i}),\qquad\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}.

(5.12)

This lower bound is no surprise, since
\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle
is nothing but the rate of the product of all the probability densities for the shreds, given they start from some
x_{i}\in W
, having length
l_{i}
and terminating at
y_{i}\in W^{\rm c}
. The
W
-admissibility of
\psi
guarantees that there is at least one way to arrange the shred-triple configurations in all the subboxes (each configuration
\mu
appears in
\approx\frac{|\Lambda_{N}|}{|W|}\psi(\mu)
subboxes) in such a way that a global loop configuration arises. A similar upper bound is generally suggesting, but here one would have to upper bound the number of such arrangements, which appears a very difficult task. Since
\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle
should be roughly of
W
-surface order and therefore negligible in the limit
R\to\infty
, we decided to control only the lower bound so explicitly. Lemma
5.5
in Section
5.3
is devoted to deriving a lower bound for the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.11
).

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

Note that the admissibility of
\psi
guarantees the existence of some
\psi_{N}\to\psi
such that
\{\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}=\psi_{N}\}
is not empty for all sufficiently large
N
. Picking
M
large enough (not depending on
N
), we also have that this event has a positive probability under
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}
, and we assume that
N
is so large. We are going to carefully decompose this event and to identify a lower bound for its probability.

We are considering only the shred-configuration part of
\Xi_{N,R}
under the PPP in
\Lambda_{N}
, that is, we neglect all loops that have particles only in one of the subboxes. In other words, we can assume that the intensity measure is equal to
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{k}\int_{\Lambda_{N}}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}(\{\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1\}\cap\cdot)
, where
\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}
is the Brownian loop measure for
k
-particle loops starting at
x
and having all leg spreads
<M
with particle-boundary condition in
\Lambda_{N}
.

From now on we drop the superscript
{\mathcal{S}}
at
\Xi_{N,R}
and write
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B}})}}}
for the empirical shred configuration measure coming from just one single path
B\in{\mathcal{C}}
, then
\Xi_{N,R}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}})}}}
if
B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{k}
is the
i
-th Brownian loop of length
k
, and
r_{k}
is the number of loops of length
k
in the Brownian loop soup. We are going to use that the PPP is realised by a bunch of
r_{k}
independent Brownian loops
B_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}},\dots,B_{r_{k}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
with
k\in\mathbb{N}
and normalised distribution, where the
r_{k}
are picked as Poisson-distributed random variables with parameter
\frac{1}{k}\int_{\Lambda}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1)
. This gives the formula

\displaystyle{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}\big(

\displaystyle\partial\Xi_{N,R}=\psi_{N}\big)={\operatorname{e}}^{-\sum_{k}\frac{1}{k}\int_{\Lambda}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1)}

(5.13)

\displaystyle\sum_{(r_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{N}}}\Big(\prod_{k}\frac{k^{-r_{k}}}{r_{k}!}\Big)\Big(\bigotimes_{k}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big)^{\otimes r_{k}}\Big)\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}})}}}=\psi_{N}\Big),

where
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}
is defined in (
5.7
) (note that the Poisson parameter
\frac{1}{k}\int_{\Lambda_{N}}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1)
is, up to the factor of
\frac{1}{k}
, equal to its total mass) and
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}
is its restriction to
\bigcap_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\{\varpi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\}
. The sum on
(r_{k})_{k}
may be restricted to those that satisfy
|\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\sum_{k}kr_{k}-\rho_{2}|\leq C\delta
for some
C
(where
\rho_{2}=\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{N}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{\partial W}\rangle
), but this restriction is implicit in the event
\{\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}})}}}\in U_{\delta}\}
.

It is easy to prove that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{k}\int_{\Lambda_{N}}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{k}\mu_{0,0}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1).

Observe that

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{k}\mu_{0,0}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1)\leq\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}},\qquad M,R\in(1,\infty),

(5.14)

for some
C_{M}\in(0,\infty)
, only depending on
M
(and on
d
and
\beta
). Indeed, from the definition of
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
in Section
5.1
and the one of
\mu_{0,0}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M}})}}}
in (
1.4
) we see that the event
\{\mathfrak{m}_{R}\geq 1\}
is empty if
k\leq R/M
, since the spreads
|B_{i\beta}-B_{(i-1)\beta}|
are
\leq M
under
\mu_{0,0}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M}})}}}
and hence only the sum on
k>R/M
remains, and the total mass of
\mu_{0,0}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M}})}}}
is
\leq Ck^{-d/2}
; we leave the details to the reader. This explains the first term on the right-hand side of (
5.11
).

Since
R>2M
, every step from a box
z+W
with size
\leq M
can terminate only in that box or its
\boxplus
-neighbouring boxes. By
\boxplus
-neighbours of the origin we mean the members of the set
\{-1,0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
; analogously we talk of
\boxplus
-neighbouring boxes in the sense of an
2R
-upscaling of this notion.

Each
B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
contributes to
\partial\Xi_{N,R}
a vector of triples
(x_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},y_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})\in W\times\mathbb{N}\times W^{\rm c}
,
j\in[m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}]
, with a distribution given by the product measure of the
p_{x_{j}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,M,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}}(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},{\rm d}y_{j})
, where the positive integers
l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
sum up over
j
to
k
, and
x_{j}=z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}+w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
and
y_{j}=z_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}+w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}+\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
, and
\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}={\operatorname{e}}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}+w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in W^{\rm c}
and
{\operatorname{e}}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=z_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}-z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
. We have to sum over all
m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in\mathbb{N}
(since
{\mathfrak{m}_{R}}\geq 1
) and all
z_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},\dots,z_{m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in Z_{N,R}
and integrate over all
w_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},\dots,w_{m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in W
such that the event
\{\partial\Xi_{N,R}=\psi_{N}\}
is met. Each
(z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}_{0},z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}_{1},\dots,z_{m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})
is a cycle, i.e.,
z^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}_{0}=z_{m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
. We may and shall assume that all the
w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
are distinct. For any
j
such that
z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in Z_{N,R}
, we know that
{\operatorname{e}}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
is a
\boxplus
-neighbour of the origin; more precisely
z_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
and
z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
are
\boxplus
-neighbours. Each
w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
has a distance
\leq M
to the boundary of
W^{\rm c}
.

Without loss of generality (see
[
B07
, Example 8.1.6(i)]
), we may and shall restrict to a
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
(the limit point of the
\psi_{N}
) that consists of finitely many atoms only, i.e., it is of the form
\psi=\sum_{\alpha\in I}s_{\alpha}\delta_{\mu_{\alpha}}
with
I
a finite set and
\sum_{\alpha}s_{\alpha}=1
and
s_{\alpha}>0
for all
\alpha\in I
, and
\mu_{\alpha}=\sum_{\gamma\in J_{\alpha}}\delta_{(x^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}_{\gamma},l^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}_{\gamma},y^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}_{\gamma})}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W}
a simple point measure. We may assume that all the
x_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}
with
\alpha\in I
and
\gamma\in J_{\alpha}
are distinct; the same for the
y_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}
.

Let us identify
\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}})}}}
for one single loop
B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
. For
z\in Z_{N,R}
, we see that
\theta_{z}(\Pi_{W_{z}}(\delta_{B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}))=\sum_{j\in[m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}]\colon z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=z}\delta_{(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})}
is equal to the shred configuration that
B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
induces in the subbox
W_{z}
. Hence, writing
{\bf w}=(w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})_{k,i,j}
and
{\bf z}=(z_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})_{k,i,j}
and
{\bf l}=(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})_{k,i,j}
, the superposition

C_{z}({\bf z},{\bf w},{\bf l})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{i\in[r_{k}]}\sum_{j\in[m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}]\colon z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=z}\delta_{(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},z+w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},z+\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})}\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{z}}

is the configuration that all the loops
B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
induce in the subbox
W_{z}
. Then

\partial\Xi_{N,R}=\sum_{k,i}\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}})}}}=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\theta_{z}(C_{z}({\bf z},{\bf w},{\bf l}))}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W}).

We call every such collection

(\mu_{z})_{z\in Z_{N,R}}=(C_{z}({\bf z},{\bf w},{\bf l}))_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\in\bigtimes_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{z}}

(5.15)

admissible
. In other words, a collection
(\mu_{z})_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\in\bigtimes_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{z}}
of configurations is called admissible if the arrangement of all these
\mu_{z}
connects up precisely at all the boundaries of all the
W_{z}
and produces a bunch of continuous loops inside
\Lambda_{N}
. With this notion, we can write the probability term on the right-hand side of (
5.13
) as follows.

\displaystyle\Big(\bigotimes_{k}

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}}\big)^{\otimes r_{k}}\Big)\Big(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}})}}}=\psi_{N}\Big)=\int_{\bigtimes_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{z}}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\big\{(\mu_{z})_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\mbox{ admissible}\big\}

(5.16)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\theta_{z}(\mu_{z})}=\psi_{N}\Big\}\,\Big(\bigotimes_{k}\big(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}}\big)^{\otimes r_{k}}\Big)\big({\rm d}((\mu_{z})_{z\in Z_{N,R}})\big).

Strictly speaking, the indicator on admissibility is superfluous, since the probability measure is concentrated on collections of
\mu_{z}
that admit only global configurations of continuous paths. But we are going to calculate now the probability by means of a product measure over
z\in Z_{N,R}
, which ignores admissibility. Only under admissibility, it gives a proper lower bound. For a upper bound, we have to upper estimate the number of admissible collections
(\mu_{z})_{z}
; the remainder is the same. For the lower bound, we rely here on the assumption of admissibility of
\psi_{N}
, which guarantees the existence of at least one admissible
(\mu_{z})_{z}
.

Let us express what
\Delta=\psi_{N}
means, where
\Delta=\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\theta_{z}(\mu_{z})}
. Since
\psi_{N}\to\psi
, and
\psi
is a simple point measure at the
\mu_{\alpha}
’s, this means that, for any small
\delta\in(0,1)
and all sufficiently large
N
and all
\alpha\in I
, we have
\Delta(B_{\delta}(\mu_{\alpha}))=\psi_{N}(B_{\delta}(\mu_{\alpha}))
, and the latter is equal to some
s_{\alpha}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}}}
that converges to
s_{\alpha}
as
N\to\infty
. We pick
\delta
so small that all the
\delta
-balls around the
x_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}
’s and the
\delta
-balls around the
y_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}
’s are pairwise disjoint. Now observe that, for any
\alpha
,

\displaystyle N_{R}\Delta(B_{\delta}(\mu_{\alpha}))

\displaystyle=\#\{z\colon\theta_{z}(\mu_{z})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\mu_{\alpha})\}

\displaystyle=\#\{z\colon\forall\gamma\in J_{\alpha}\exists(k,i,j)\colon z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=z,w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}),l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=l_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}},\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(y_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}})\}.

Note that, for any
\alpha\in I
and
\gamma\in J_{\alpha}
and all
(k,i)
, the index
j
in the description of the set is unique. We denote the set of all
({\bf z},{\bf l},{\bf w})
having the property on the right-hand side by
A
.

Recall the notation for certain densities
p_{x}(l,\cdot)
from (
5.1
) and (
5.5
) (with various super-indices). Now using Lemma
5.1
and the independence of all the
B^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}
, we can write (conceiving
p_{x}(l,\cdot)
as a measure instead of a density)

\displaystyle\Big(\bigotimes_{k}

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,\Lambda,{\rm par}}})}}}\big)^{\otimes r_{k}}\Big)\Big(\frac{1}{N_{R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\mu_{z}}=\psi_{N}\,\Big|\,(\mu_{z})_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\mbox{ admissible}\Big)

(5.17)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bf z,l}\int{\rm d}{\bf w}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{A}({\bf z,l,w})\,\prod_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\prod_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i\in[r_{k}]}\prod_{j\in[m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}]\colon z=z_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M,\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{z+w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},{\rm d}(z+\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})).

We may replace the
p
-term by
p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}_{w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},{\rm d}\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})
, since every leg that starts from
W_{z}
has spread
\leq M
and hence this term is invariant under shift by
z
. For these
z
, we obtain a lower bound by estimating

p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}_{w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},{\rm d}\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}))=p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}_{w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}(l_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}},{\rm d}({\operatorname{e}}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}+w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}))\geq\inf_{x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}})}p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}_{x}(l_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}},{\rm d}({\operatorname{e}}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}+w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})),

(5.18)

if
(k,i,j)
is such that
w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}),l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}=l_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}},w_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(y_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}})
.

We further have

\displaystyle\mbox{l.h.s.~of }\eqref{probrepre}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bf z,l}\int{\rm d}{\bf w}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{A}({\bf z,l,w})\,\prod_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\prod_{i\in[r_{k}]}\prod_{j\in[m^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}]}p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}_{w_{j-1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}}}(l_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}},{\rm d}\widetilde{w}_{j}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({i,k}})}}})

(5.19)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{\alpha\in I}\Big(\prod_{\gamma\in J_{\alpha}}\inf_{x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}})}p^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}_{x}(l_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}},{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(y_{\gamma}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\alpha}})}}}))\Big)^{s_{\alpha}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}}}N_{R}}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{e}}^{-N_{R}{\mathcal{O}}(\delta)}\exp\Big(N_{R}\int\psi({\rm d}\mu)\,\log\mathbb{P}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\partial W,M}}})}}}_{\inf}({\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\mu))\Big),

where
\mathbb{P}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\partial W,M}}})}}}_{\inf}
is defined by

\mathbb{P}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\partial W,M}})}}}_{\inf}({\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\mu))=\prod_{i\in I}\inf_{x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x_{i})}p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l_{i},{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(y_{i})),\qquad{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\mu)=\bigtimes_{i\in I}\big[{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(x_{i})\times\{l_{i}\}\times{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(y_{i})\big],

(5.20)

where
\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(z)
is the ball with radius
\delta
around
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Note that this lower bound does not depend on
(r_{k})_{k}
. For the lower bound, one can pick one choice of
(r_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and lower estimate the sum over
(r_{k})_{k}
against this single summand. This implies, for some
C\in(0,\infty)
,

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}\big(\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}=\psi_{N}\big)\geq-\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}}-\frac{C\delta}{|W|}+\frac{1}{|W|}\int\psi({\rm d}\mu)\,\log\mathbb{P}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\partial W,M}}})}}}_{\inf}({\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\mu)).

Since the left-hand side does not depend on
\delta
, we can make
\delta\downarrow 0
on the right-hand side and obtain the assertion in (
5.11
).
∎

We now give an existence assertion for admissible boundary-shred configurations
\psi
in a setting that will be relevant in Section
9
when we finish the proof of the lower bound in Theorem
2.5
. We keep using the notation that we reminded on at the beginning of Section
5
. We call a measure
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})

ergodic with respect to

W_{R}
or simply
W_{R}
-ergodic
, if it is
W_{R}
-shift invariant (i.e.,
\theta_{x}(P)=P
for
x\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
) and ergodic under shifts by vectors in
2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Lemma 5.4

(
W_{R}
-admissible
\psi
’s)
.

Fix
R\in(0,\infty)
and put
W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}
. Pick
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
and assume that
P
is
W
-ergodic. Then
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
is
W
-admissible.

Proof.

Put
\xi=\Pi_{W}(P)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W})\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
. Extending the definition (
4.20
) to loops and interlacements, we conceive
\Xi_{N,R}=\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega,\varpi}})}}}
as the empirical measure coming from loops and interlacements in
\Lambda_{N}
(and
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}
is the loop configuration and
\varpi\in{\mathcal{S}}
the interlacement configuration). Indeed, it is an empirical measure (discrete mixture) of
W
-shifted copies of
\Pi_{W}(\omega,\varpi)
, which have distribution
\xi
under
P({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))
. Here we use the shift-invariance of
P
, more precisely only the invariance under shifts by vectors
\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

According to the ergodic theorem for
P
, for any open neighbourhood
U(\xi)\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
of
\xi
,

\lim_{N\to\infty}P(\Xi_{N,R}\in U(\xi))=1.

In particular, the event
\{(\omega,\varpi)\in{\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}}\colon\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega,\varpi}})}}}_{N,R}\in U(\xi)\}
is non-empty for all sufficiently large
N
. This implies that, for any
N
, there is a configuration
(\omega_{N},\varpi_{N})\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\Lambda_{N}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{\Lambda_{N}}
(namely
\omega_{N}=\Pi_{\Lambda_{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega+\varpi)
and
\varpi_{N}=\Pi_{\Lambda_{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega+\varpi)
) such that
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{N},\varpi_{N}}})}}}
(which is equal to
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega,\varpi}})}}}_{N,R}
) lies in
{\mathcal{U}}(\xi)
. From
(\omega_{N},\varpi_{N})
, we can construct a loop configuration
\widetilde{\omega}_{N}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\Lambda_{N}}
as follows. We keep the loop configuration
\omega_{N}
unchanged and modify all the shreds in
\varpi_{N}
by concatenating all
\Lambda_{N}
-shreds to a loop by removing the last leg (the only one that leaves
\Lambda_{N}
) and adding a piece from its termination site in
\Lambda_{N}
to its initial site in
\Lambda_{N}
that runs within
\Lambda_{N}
. It is easily seen that the above modification can be done in such a way that the distance between
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{N},\varpi_{N}}})}}}
and
\Xi_{\widetilde{N},R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\widetilde{\omega}_{N}}})}}}
vanishes as
N\to\infty
, so
\Xi_{\widetilde{N},R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\widetilde{\omega}_{N}}})}}}
lies also in
U(\xi)
for all large
N
.

Now let
{\mathcal{B}}(\psi)
be some open neighbourhood of
\psi
, and pick the neighbourhood
{\mathcal{B}}(\xi)
in such a way that
\{\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi^{\prime})\colon\xi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}(\xi)\}\subset{\mathcal{B}}(\psi)
. Therefore, for any large
N
,
\partial\Xi_{\widetilde{N},R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\widetilde{\omega}_{N},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
lies in
{\mathcal{B}}(\psi)
. Hence
\psi
is admissible.
∎

5.3.
Estimating the LDP lower bound

In this section, we give in Lemma
5.5
a further lower bound for the LDP lower bound of Lemma
5.3
, which shows that it is negligible for all the (approximations of)
\psi
’s that we will later apply it to. Furthermore, we use this in order to prove that the expected shred number in a large box under a shift-invariant interlacement-configuration distribution is negligible with respect to the volume of the box, see Corollary
5.8
, which needs the preparation in Lemma
5.7
.

The idea in the proof of Lemma
5.5
is that we will be able to control
\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle
(see (
5.12
)) only for
\psi
’s that are concentrated on shred configurations in
W_{R}
with legs that have a bounded spread, whose shreds have a bounded spread-out behaviour on longer scales, and that produce a bounded number of particles in any subbox of a given size. More precisely, with large parameters
M\in(1,\infty)
and
\vartheta,S\in\mathbb{N}
, introduce the set of configurations of shreds whose legs have a spread
<M
and which satisfy an upper bound for subpieces of length
S
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\sum_{g\in\Gamma}\delta_{g}\in{\mathcal{S}}\colon\forall g\in\Gamma,\forall i\in[\ell(g)],\|g_{i}\|_{\rm sp}<M,

(5.21)

\displaystyle\forall g\in\Gamma,\forall j\in[\lfloor\textstyle{\frac{1}{S}}\ell(g)\rfloor],g(((jS)\wedge\ell(g))\beta)-g((j-1)S\beta)|\leq\vartheta\sqrt{S}\Big\},

where we remind that
g\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell(g)}
is a shred of length
\ell(g)
. Write
{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}({\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}})
and
A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}=A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
, where
\Theta=(M,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta,S)
, then we will assume that
\psi
is concentrated on
A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}
.

Lemma 5.5

(Lower estimates for expectations of
\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}
)
.

For any
R,M\in(1,\infty)
, fix a shred configuration measure
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
.

(1)

There is a
C_{M}
(only depending on
M
,
d
and
\beta
) such that, for any
R,M\in(1,\infty)
and any shred configuration measure
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
,

-\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi),\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle\leq C_{M}\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle.

(5.22)

(2)

There are constants
C_{M,\mathfrak{r},T}
(only depending on
M,\mathfrak{r},T
and
d
,
\beta
) and
C
(depending only on
d
and
\beta
) such that, for any
R,M,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta\in(0,\infty)
and
S\in\mathbb{N}
and for any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}})
that is concentrated on
A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{R};\Theta}
, where
\Theta=(M,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta,S)
,

-\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi),\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle\leq\Big[\frac{C_{M,\mathfrak{r},T}}{R}+\frac{C\vartheta^{2}}{S}\Big]\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle.

(5.23)

Proof.

Let us first derive the lower bound in (
5.24
) for
p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l,i)
, also using that
|x-y|\leq lM
and that
y\in W_{R+M}\setminus W_{R}
and
x\in W_{R}\setminus W_{R-M}
for any
(x,l,y)
in the support of any
\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
in the support of
\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
.

Recall that
\tau\beta\in\beta\mathbb{N}
is the first time
\in\beta\mathbb{N}
at which the shred leaves
W
. The steps of the underlying random walk
(B_{k\beta})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
are Gaussian, conditioned on being
<M
, and have therefore a density that is bounded from below (by some
M
-depending constant). Therefore, for any
(x,l,y)\in(W_{R}\setminus W_{R-M})\times\mathbb{N}\times(W_{R+M}\setminus W_{R})
, using the Markov property at time
\beta(l-1)
, writing
g_{M}
for the Gaussian density restricted to
B_{M}(0)
,

\displaystyle p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l,y)

\displaystyle\geq\int_{W\cap B_{M}(y)}\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}(\tau>l-1,B_{\beta(l-1)}\in{\rm d}w)g_{M}(w-y)

\displaystyle\geq C_{M}\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}(\tau>l-1,B_{\beta(l-1)}\in W\cap B_{M}(y)).

Now we distinguish the cases where
l\gg R^{2}
(where the long stay of the walk in
W
is a late-escape deviation),
\varepsilon|x-y|^{2}\leq l\leq CR^{2}
(where the walk has so much time to arrive in
W\cap B_{M}(y)
that the CLT can be applied) and
\frac{|x-y|}{M}\leq l\ll|x-y|^{2}
(where the sprint to
W\cap B_{M}(y)
is a large or moderate deviation, since it makes very long steps).

Indeed, if
l\geq CR^{2}
for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, then we can estimate
\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}(\tau>l-1,B_{\beta(l-1)}\in W\cap B_{M}(y))
from below against
{\operatorname{e}}^{-cl/R^{2}}C_{M}R^{1-d}
since the probability for a long path to stay in a given box decays exponentially in the length of the path divided by the square of the radius of the box, according to Brownian scaling; and since the endpoint of such a path has a positive density in
W_{R}\setminus W_{R-M}
, which has volume
\geq C_{M}R^{d-1}
. (This asymptotics may be made much more precise with the help of the principal eigenvalue of the Laplace operator in
W_{R}
, which is
\sim C/R^{2}
, and the corresponding eigenfunction, but this would be cumbersome.)

Second, if
\varepsilon|x-y|^{2}\leq l\leq CR^{2}
for some small
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, then, on the event
\{\tau>l-1\}
, the path
(B_{\beta k})_{k\in\{0,1,\dots,l-1\}}
behaves like a Brownian motion with diffusive constant
\asymp l
and arrives in
B_{M}(y)
with probability
\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{-c|x-y|^{2}/l}C_{M}
, according to Donsker’s invariance principle, where the factor
C_{M}
expresses the volume of
B_{M}(y)
. (Another explanation of the term
{\operatorname{e}}^{-c|x-y|^{2}/l}
is that the Brownian motion path can be divided into
l
steps of length
\approx|x-y|/l
each.)

Furthermore, if we consider the case
\frac{|x-y|}{M}\leq l\leq\varepsilon|x-y|^{2}
, then we estimate
\mathbb{P}_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}(\tau>l-1,B_{\beta(l-1)}\in W\cap B_{M}(y))\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{-c|x-y|^{2}/l}
, according to a large-deviation principle (if
l\asymp|x-y|
), respectively a moderate-deviation principle (if
|x-y|\ll l\leq\varepsilon|x-y|^{2}
). Indeed, the walk has to make
\asymp l
steps of sizes
\asymp\frac{|x-y|}{l}
, and the probability for making such large steps is approximately Gaussian.

Summarizing the second and third case, we obtain

\log p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,M}})}}}(l,y)\geq\begin{cases}-c\frac{l}{R^{2}}+\log(C_{M}R^{1-d}),&\mbox{if }l\geq CR^{2},\\
-c\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{l}-C_{M},&\mbox{if }\frac{|x-y|}{M}\leq l\leq CR^{2}.\end{cases}

(5.24)

For an arbitrary
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
, abbreviate
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
. Then the left-hand side of (
5.22
) or (
5.23
) is an integral with respect to
\psi({\rm d}\mu)
, where
\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
.
Hence, it can be estimated from above against

\int\psi({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i\in I}\Big[\big(c\textstyle{\frac{l_{i}}{R^{2}}}-\log(C_{M}R^{1-d})\big){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{l\geq CR^{2}\}+\big(c\textstyle{\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{l_{i}}}+C_{M}\big){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\textstyle{\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|}{M}}\leq l_{i}\leq CR^{2}\}\Big].

(5.25)

Estimating
c\frac{l_{i}}{R^{2}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{l\geq CR^{2}\}\leq c\frac{l_{i}}{R^{2}}
and using in the next term that
{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{l\geq CR^{2}\}\leq\frac{l}{CR^{2}}
