, the first part can be estimated, for any large
R
, against

\frac{C_{M}}{R}\int\psi({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i\in I}l_{i}=\frac{C_{M}}{R}\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle.

Furthermore, we can simply estimate the second part against
C_{M}l_{i}
and obtain the bound in (
5.22
).

Now assume that
\xi
is concentrated on
A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}
. We recall that
\sum_{i\in I}\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{l_{i}}
is indeed integrated with respect to
\xi
, under which
|f_{i}(jS\beta)-f_{i}((j+1)S\beta)|\leq\vartheta\sqrt{S}
holds for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and for any
i\in I
, where we recall that we wrote
f_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{i}}
for the
i
-th shred (with
f_{i}(0)=x_{i}
and
f_{i}(\ell(f_{i})\beta)=y_{i}
), such that we can estimate
|x_{i}-y_{i}|\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\lceil l_{i}/S\rceil}|f_{i}(jS\beta)-f_{i}((j-1)S\beta)|\leq\frac{\vartheta l_{i}}{\sqrt{S}}
. Therefore, the second part is upper bounded against

\displaystyle\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}\Big[c\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{l_{i}}+C_{M}\Big]\leq\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)\,\sum_{i\in I}\Big[C\frac{\vartheta^{2}l_{i}}{S}+C_{M}\Big]=\frac{C\vartheta^{2}}{S}\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle+C_{M}\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)\,|I|.

Recall that
\xi
is concentrated on
A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{R};\Theta}
, which in particular means that in any of the boxes
z+W_{\mathfrak{r}}
there are no more than
T
particles in the shreds and each leg has spread
<M
with
\xi
-probability one. This implies that, with
\xi
-probability one, there are no more than of surface order (i.e.,
O(R^{d-1})
) of shreds, and the proportionality factor depends on
M,\mathfrak{r}
and
T
. This shows that
C_{M}\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)\,|I|\leq C_{M,\mathfrak{r},T}R^{d-1}
for some constant that depends only on
M
,
\mathfrak{r}
, and
T
(and on
d
and on
\beta
). Finally, we absorb the
C_{M}
from above in
C_{M,\mathfrak{r},T}
, which finishes the proof of (
5.23
).
∎

Remark 5.6

(Simpler estimates for Brownian bridges)

It is clear that, under shred-configuration measures
\xi
whose shreds are independent Brownian bridges (for example
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
), there are much simpler upper bounds for
-\langle\xi,\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R},M}\rangle
, but the main point in Lemma
5.5
is that we need to control expectations under pretty arbitrary
\xi
s and therefore need to put the condition that
all
of them lie in
{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
under
\xi
. Indeed, if all the shreds under
\xi
are Brownian bridges, then the only term in the above proof that needs and offers a simpler argument is
\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)\,\sum_{i\in I}\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{l_{i}}
, which is easily estimated by estimating
\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{l_{i}}\leq M\sum_{j=1}^{l_{i}/S}|f_{i}(jS\beta)-f_{i}((j+1)S\beta)|
, which gives, by the Gaussian nature of
f_{i}(jS\beta)-f_{i}((j+1)S\beta)
,

\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)\,\sum_{i\in I}\frac{|x_{i}-y_{i}|^{2}}{l_{i}}\leq MC\sqrt{S}\int\xi({\rm d}\varpi)\,\sum_{i\in I}\frac{l_{i}}{S}\leq\frac{C_{M}}{\sqrt{S}}\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle.

\Diamond

Let us append now some useful estimate for the expected number of interlacement-shreds of shift-invariant interlacement configuration measures in a large box. Together with Lemma
5.5
, it will lead to a flexible and useful bound in Corollary
5.8
below.

Lemma 5.7

(Estimating the expected number of interlacement-shreds)
.

There is a
C\in(0,\infty)
such that, for any
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{S}})
, and for any
R\in\mathbb{N}
,

\int\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,|\mu|\leq\frac{C|W_{R}|}{R}\Big(J_{W_{R}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))+\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\rangle-\frac{1}{|W_{R}|}\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P),\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R}}\rangle\Big),

(5.26)

where we recall the definition of
\mathfrak{p}
from (
5.12
) (with
M=\infty
) and (
5.5
).

Additionally, if
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
for some
M\in(1,\infty)
, then (
5.26
) holds with
\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R}}
replaced by
\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R},M}
.

Proof.

We write
W
instead of
W_{R}
. We claim that there is a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
such that

\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,|\mu|\leq\frac{C}{R}\int_{{\mathcal{S}}}P({\rm d}\varpi)\,\sum_{g}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{g(0)\in W\}|g(\beta)-g(0)|,\qquad R\in\mathbb{N},

(5.27)

where
\sum_{g}
is the sum on all legs in the entire interlacement configuration (there are no loops).
In other words, the expected number of
W
-shreds under
\Pi_{W}(P)
is not larger than
C/R
times the expected sum of all jump lengths of all the legs with starting sites in
W
. Indeed, our argument for this is as follows. Fix one of the
2d
hyperplanes that define the boundaries of
W
, say
H=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\colon x_{1}=R\}
. Consider all the
W
-shreds that make a final jump over this hyperplane (i.e., with a starting site with first index
\in[-R,R)
and a target site with first index in
(R,\infty)
). Put
I_{x}=[x,x+1)\times[-R,R]^{d-1}
and call
M(x,y)
the number of jumps of any leg in the configuration from
I_{x}
into
I_{y}
. For
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, consider
M_{z}=\sum_{x=z-R}^{z-1}\sum_{y=z}^{\infty}M(x,y)
, then the the number of shreds with final jump over
H
is not larger than
M_{R}
. By shift-invariance of
P
, the expectation of
M_{z}
does not depend on
z
. Hence, the expected number of
W
-shreds that make a final jump over
H
is not larger than
C/R
times the expectation of

\sum_{z=-R}^{R}M_{z}=\sum_{x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}\colon-R\leq x\leq z\leq y,z\leq R}M(x,y)\leq\sum_{x=-R}^{R}\sum_{y\geq x}|y-x|M(x,y)\leq\sum_{g}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{g(0)\in W\}|g(\beta)-g(0)|,

where
\sum_{g}
is the sum over all legs
\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
in the entire configuration. Note that the expectation of
M_{z}
(i.e., the one of
M_{R}
) is not smaller than the left-hand side of (
5.27
). Summing over all the
2d
hyperplanes, our argument implies (
5.27
).

Now we estimate the expectation on the right-hand side of (
5.27
) by using (
2.7
). We write the sum over the legs as
\sum_{i,k}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{f_{i}((k-1)\beta)\in W\}|f_{i}(k\beta)-f_{i}((k-1)\beta)|
, where
\varpi=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{f_{i}}
is the shred configuration in
W
. We abbreviate the kernel
(\Pi_{W}(P))_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(\mu,\cdot)
by
P(\mu,\cdot)
and see (by first considering a cut-off version of
\sum_{i,k}|f_{i}(k\beta)-f_{i}((k-1)\beta)|
) that

\displaystyle\int

\displaystyle\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\varpi)\,\sum_{i,k}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{f_{i}((k-1)\beta)\in W\}|f_{i}(k\beta)-f_{i}((k-1)\beta)|

(5.28)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,\int_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}P(\mu,{\rm d}\varpi)\sum_{i,k}|f_{i}(k\beta)-f_{i}((k-1)\beta)|

\displaystyle\leq\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,\Big[H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(P(\mu,\cdot)\mid{\tt K}_{W}(\mu,\cdot))+\log\int{\tt K}_{W}(\mu,{\rm d}\varpi)\Big(\prod_{i,k}{\operatorname{e}}^{|f_{i}(k\beta)-f_{i}((k-1)\beta)|}\Big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq|W|J_{W}(\Pi_{W}(P))+\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i}\log\int q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,l_{i}}})}}}({\rm d}f)\prod_{k=1}^{l_{i}}{\operatorname{e}}^{|f(k\beta)-f((k-1)\beta)|},

where we recall the definition of
{\tt K}_{W}
in (
2.5
), and we wrote
\mu=\sum_{i}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
, as usual. Recalling the definition of
p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}(l,y)
from (
5.5
) and (
5.6
) (both with
M=\infty
) and writing
g_{\beta}
for the Gaussian density with variance
2\beta
, we further estimate

\displaystyle\int q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,l_{i}}})}}}({\rm d}f)\prod_{k=1}^{l_{i}}{\operatorname{e}}^{|f(k\beta)-f((k-1)\beta)|}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p_{x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}(l_{i},y_{i})}\int_{W^{l_{i}-1}}{\rm d}w_{1}\dots{\rm d}w_{l_{i}-1}\,\prod_{k=1}^{l_{i}-1}\big[{\operatorname{e}}^{|w_{k}-w_{k-1}|}g_{\beta}(w_{k}-w_{k-1})\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p_{x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}(l_{i},y_{i})}C^{l_{i}-1}C^{\prime},

with
C=\log\mathbb{E}_{0}[{\operatorname{e}}^{|B_{\beta}|}]
and
C^{\prime}=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}[{\operatorname{e}}^{|x|}g_{\beta}(x)]
, and we wrote
w_{0}=x_{i}
and
w_{l_{i}}=y_{i}
. We estimate the
C
-terms against
C^{l_{i}}
(using our convention that
C
is a generic constant) and see that the last term on the right-hand side of (
5.28
) is not larger than
\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i}[Cl_{i}-\log p_{x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}(l_{i},y_{i})]=C\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle-\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i\in I}\log p_{x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}(l_{i},y_{i})
. Now use the shift-invariance of
P
to see that
\frac{1}{|W_{R}|}\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle=\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
, which finishes the proof (recalling (
5.12
) for the second term).
∎

As a consequence, here is an upper bound for the expected number of interlacement-shreds in a large box
W_{R}
that vanishes as
R\to\infty
for every shift-invariant measure
P
that has finite particle density and finite entropy
{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
(thanks to (
2.16
)).

Corollary 5.8

(Estimating the expected number of interlacement-shreds)
.

For any
M\in(1,\infty)
, there is a
C_{M}\in(1,\infty)
such that, for any
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
and any sufficiently large
R
,

\frac{1}{|W_{R}|}\int\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\rm d}\mu)\,|\mu|\leq\frac{1}{R}\Big(J_{W_{R}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))+C_{M}\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}},\ell}})}}}\rangle\Big).

Proof.

In (
5.26
) for
M<\infty
, use (
5.22
) with
\xi=\Pi_{W_{R}}(P)
for estimating the last term and use the shift-invariance of
P
.
∎

6.
Large deviation analysis: making
N\to\infty

In this section, we derive the necessary LDP-framework for the thermodynamic limit as
N\to\infty
for the empirical measure
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P}}})}}}_{N,R}
defined at the beginning of Section
4.4
, where
\omega_{\rm P}
is the marked PPP under
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
. In Section
6.1
we give first some technical preparation and then the basic large deviation principle that we need (see Corollary
6.5
) and in Section
6.2
some crucial properties of the rate function,
J_{W}
, which will be important for the proofs of the main results, Theorems
2.5
and (
2.3
). In order to apply later the upper bound of that LDP, we need to derive some crucial assertions about compactness, which we do in Section
6.3
. In Section
6.4
we apply it to derive an upper bound for the limit superior as
N\to\infty
of
\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}
times the logarithm of the right-hand side of (
4.25
); in Section
6.5
we give a lower bound for the limit inferior of
\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}
times the logarithm of the right-hand side of (
4.39
).

As always, we assume that the situation of Section
1.4
is given, i.e., the regular decomposition of the centred box
\Lambda_{N}
into subboxes of radius
R
, which possibly depends on
N
and converges to a given
R\in\mathbb{N}
. Recall the notion of a relative entropy from (
2.6
).

6.1.
The conditional large-deviation principle

The empirical measure
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega_{\rm P}}})}}}_{N,R}
defined in (
4.20
) is a mixture over
z\in Z_{N,R}
of the
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
-valued random variable pairs
(\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}),\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}))
defined in (
4.26
), and we consider the distribution under the PPP
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
. The first member of these pairs comes from those loops that are entirely contained in some of these subboxes, while the second one, the shred part, comes from those loops that are
R
-crossing. If these pairs would be i.i.d. over
z
and their distribution would not depend on
N
, then Sanov’s theorem (see
[
DZ10
, Section ]
) would immediately give the validity of the LDP with an explicit rate function. But the shred-parts are far from being i.i.d., and the distribution of the pairs depends on
N
(namely via the boundary condition).

Hence, we need to take care of these two obstacles. The second one is only technical: because of the boundary condition (periodic or particle) in
\Lambda_{N}
, the distribution in the subboxes depend slightly on
N
and on
z
. We will handle this problem with the help of the concept of exponential equivalence (which says that two exponentially equivalent sequences satisfy the same LDP, if any, see
[
DZ10
, Section 4.2.2]
) and will be using the following technical lemma, whose proof is straight-forward and is left to the reader.

Lemma 6.1

(Perturbed empirical measures are exponentially equivalent)
.

On a Polish space
{\mathcal{X}}
, assume that an i.i.d. sequence
(X_{z})_{z\in[N]}
is given and another sequence
(X_{z}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}}})_{z\in[N]}
that satisfies

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{z=1}^{N}\log\mathbb{E}\big[{\operatorname{e}}^{f(X_{z}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}}})}\big]=\log\mathbb{E}[{\operatorname{e}}^{f(X_{1})}],\qquad f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm b}({\mathcal{X}}).

(6.1)

Then the empirical measures
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{z=1}^{N}\delta_{X_{z}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({N}})}}}}
and
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{z=1}^{N}\delta_{X_{z}}
are exponentially equivalent.

In order to apply this, we need to prove that the distributions of the pairs satisfy the criterion in (
6.1
).
Recall the Brownian kernel
{\tt K}_{W}
defined in (
2.5
) and introduce also the version
{\tt K}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}(\mu,\cdot)\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W})
, where
q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W}})}}}
is replaced by
q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W,\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
; see (
5.3
). This measure is concentrated on the set of those collections
(f_{i})_{i}
of
W
-shreds such that
f_{i}
is a length-
l_{i}
shred with
f_{i}(0)=x_{i}
and
f_{i}(l_{i}\beta)=y_{i}
for any
i\in I
.

For any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W})
and any
W\Subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we denote by
\xi_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}
the kernel from
{\mathcal{T}}_{W}
into
{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
that is defined by letting
\xi_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(\mu,\cdot)
be a regular conditional version of
\xi
given
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)=\mu
for
\mu\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W}
. Then
\xi
is equal to
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes\xi_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}
.

Lemma 6.2

(The shred-configuration kernel)
.

We consider either periodic or particle boundary condition.

(1)

For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
and any
z\in Z_{N,R}
, the map
{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{z}}\times{\mathcal{G}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}\ni(\mu,{\rm d}\xi)\mapsto{\tt K}_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}(\mu,{\rm d}\xi)
is equal to the kernel
\Pi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}({{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}})_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{z}}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{z}}}
, where we recall that
W_{z}=z+W
.

(2)

The empirical measure of the kernels from (1) converges to the kernel
{\tt K}_{W}
in the following sense. Assume that
\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\mu_{z}}\to\delta_{\mu}
in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
as
N\to\infty
, then

\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}}\big[{\operatorname{e}}^{f(\theta_{z}(\Pi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}))}\big]=\log{\tt K}_{W}\Big(\mu,{\operatorname{e}}^{f(\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}})}\Big),\qquad f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm b}({\mathcal{S}}_{W}),

(6.2)

where
\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{z}}[\cdot]={\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}[\cdot\mid\theta_{z}(\partial\Pi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}})=\mu_{z}]
.

(3)

Both under
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}
and
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm per}}})}}}
, under the assumption that
\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\mu_{z}}\to\delta_{\mu}
in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
as
N\to\infty
, the
pairs
(\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}),\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}))
,
z\in Z_{N,R}
satisfy (
6.1
).

Proof.

(1) Lemma
5.1
(1) implies that
{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},\rm bc}})}}}
, conditional on the family
(\mu_{z})_{z\in Z_{N,R}}=(\theta_{z}(\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}(\omega_{\rm P})))_{z\in Z_{N,R}}
of the boundary shred configurations, the shred-configuration family
(\theta_{z}(\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{z}}(\omega_{\rm P})))_{z\in Z_{N,R}}
has the distribution
\bigotimes_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{\tt K}_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},\rm bc}})}}}(\mu_{z},\cdot)
. By superposition of all the independent loops of the loop soup, it implies also that, for any
z
, the conditional distribution of the family of shred configurations in
W_{z}
given the boundary shred configuration
\mu_{z}
is given by the product measure of the
q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W_{z},\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
.

(2) For ‘
\rm bc
’ equal to particle boundary condition, according to Lemma
5.1
(2), the boundary condition has no effect, since all particles involved in the definition of
q
lie in
W
anyway, i.e.,
q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}=q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}
. Hence, by Assertion (1), (
6.2
) is trivially true without taking the limit. However, for periodic boundary condition, one needs to prove that
{\tt K}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}(\mu,\cdot)
is close to
{\tt K}_{W}(\mu,\cdot)
, i.e.,
q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W,\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
is close to
q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W}})}}}
in the sense of (
6.2
). We leave this to the reader, see the hints in the proof of (3).

(3) Observe that, since the empirical measures of the
\mu_{z}
are given and converge, the loop part and the shred part are independent, and the necessary assertion for the shred part is in (2). The proof for the corresponding loop part is similar, hence we restrict ourselves to giving just some hints and leave the details to the reader.

Note that we can estimate, for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, only depending on
R
(and on
\beta
and on
d
), for any sufficiently large
N
,

p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,\Lambda_{N},{\rm per}}})}}}(l,y)\leq p_{x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W}})}}}(l,y)\big[1+{\operatorname{e}}^{-CN^{2/d}/l}\big],\qquad x\in W,l\in\mathbb{N},y\in W^{\rm c}.

(6.3)

The reason for this is the following. According to
[
BR97
, Theorem 6.3.8]
, there are, for any
K\in(0,\infty)
, constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
such that the solution
g^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda,\rm per}})}}}_{t}(x,y)
to the heat equation with the Laplace operator with periodic boundary condition in the box
\Lambda
satisfies

0\leq g^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda,\rm per}})}}}_{t}(x,y)-g_{t}(x,y)\leq C_{1}g_{t/C_{2}}(x,y)\,{\operatorname{e}}^{-C_{3}{\operatorname{dist}}(y,\partial\Lambda)^{2}/t},\qquad x,y\in\Lambda,t\in(0,K],

(6.4)

where
g_{t}
denotes the free fundamental solution, i.e., the standard Gaussian kernel with variance
2t
. We leave the remainder of the details to the reader.
∎

Let us turn to the first of the two obstacles. This is more serious. Indeed, the first members of the pairs are independent over
z
and (ignoring the small dependence on
z
and
N
) also identically distributed. But the second ones are not at all, since neighbouring subboxes have a strong dependence, and this dependence has very long correlations. Nevertheless, there is a way out of that, and this is a conditioning on the boundary configurations. The idea is that, under this conditioning, the configurations inside the boxes are independent with a distribution that depends only on the boundary configuration (by the virtue of the Markov property of the Brownian motion). More precisely, we condition on the boundary empirical measure of
\Xi_{N,R}
, namely

\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,R}\big)=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\theta_{z}\varpi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}})}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})

(6.5)

and obtain that
\Xi_{N,R}
is indeed an empirical measure of independent random variables, and we also have sufficient information about their distributions.

In order to catch the large deviation behaviour of the conditional distribution of
\Xi_{N,R}
, we need to employ an LDP of the following type. The following LDP is taken from
[
PRV13
, Thm. 17]
; it goes back to an unpublished manuscript by C. Léonard; see
[
ADPZ11
, Thm. 1]
for a conditional version of this LDP.

Lemma 6.3

(Sanov-type LDP for type-dependent independent random variables)
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Y}}
be two Polish spaces and pick
\rho\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{X}})
and let
(x_{i}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in
{\mathcal{X}}
such that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{i}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}}}
converges weakly towards
\rho
as
n\to\infty
. Let
\zeta\colon{\mathcal{X}}\times\sigma({\mathcal{Y}})\to[0,1]
be a continuous Markov kernel from
{\mathcal{X}}
to
{\mathcal{Y}}
, where
\sigma({\mathcal{Y}})
denotes the Borel sigma field on
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Furthermore, let
(Y_{i}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}})_{i\in[n]}
have the distribution
\bigotimes_{i\in[n]}\zeta(x_{i}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}},\cdot)
. Then the empirical pair measure
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(x_{i}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}},Y_{i}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}})}
satisfies an LDP with rate function

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\ni q\mapsto\begin{cases}H_{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}(q\,|\,\rho\otimes\zeta)&\mbox{if }\pi_{1}(q)=\rho,\\
+\infty&\mbox{otherwise,}\end{cases}

(6.6)

where
\pi_{1}
is the canonical projection
{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{X}}
, and
\pi_{1}(q)
is the corresponding image measure.

We recall that
\rho\otimes\zeta
is defined by
\rho\otimes\zeta({\rm d}(x,y))=\rho({\rm d}x)\zeta(x,{\rm d}y)
.

Based on Lemma
6.3
, we can give a conditional LDP for
\Xi_{N,R}
for a fixed box
W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}
. We need to take care of a consistency property (or marginal property) that
\Xi_{N,R}
asymptotically has: the rate function is equal to
\infty
in any configuration measures
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
that cannot be extended to the larger box
3^{k}W
for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that the extension is
W
-shift invariant (meaning that projection of the extension to any subbox
W_{z}=z+W
of
3^{k}W
,
z\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, is equal to
\xi
after shifting back to
W
. (This property is analogous to the marginal property of the rate function for the empirical measures
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\delta_{(M_{k-1},M_{k})}
of a Markov chain
(M_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, whose two marginal measures are equal in the limit
N\to\infty
, and therefore the rate function is equal to
\infty
if this property is not satisfied; see
[
DZ10
, Section 3.1.3]
.) Here is a precise definition. We introduce the set
{\mathcal{R}}_{3^{k},W}
of rectangles
\subset 3^{k}W
of the form
\bigcup_{z\in 2RZ}z+W
for some
Z\subset\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Then we introduce the set of
W
-shift invariant configurations in
3^{k}W
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W})\colon\forall\widetilde{W}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3^{k},W}\forall z\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}\colon

(6.7)

\displaystyle z+\widetilde{W}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3^{k},W}\Longrightarrow\theta_{z}(\Pi_{z+\widetilde{W}}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}))=\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})\Big\}

and the set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})

\displaystyle=\big\{\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})\colon\exists\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\colon\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})=\xi\big\}=\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}),

(6.8)

where we remind that
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W})\to{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
is the well-known projection operator from (
4.18
), here with domain
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W})
.

Lemma 6.4

(LDP for
\Xi_{N,R}
, conditional on
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Xi_{N,R})
)
.

Fix a window
W=[-R,R]^{d}
and any
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
. Then, under
{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
conditioned on
\{\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Xi_{N,R})=\psi_{N}\}
for some
\psi_{N}\to\psi
, the sequence
(\Xi_{N,R})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies an LDP on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
with speed
|\Lambda_{N}|
with rate function

J_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\psi}})}}}(\xi)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{|W|}H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi\,\big|\,\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\psi\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]\big)&\mbox{if }\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)=\psi\mbox{ and }\xi\in\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}),\\
+\infty&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(6.9)

Proof.

First we show that the rate function is
\infty
outside
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Clearly,
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
is closed, since all the projection operators appearing in the definition of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
are continuous.

Consider the measure

\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}(\omega)=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\delta_{\theta_{z}(\Pi_{z+3^{k}W}(\omega))}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W}),\qquad\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}}.

(6.10)

This is the empirical measure of the loop/shred configuration in the boxes
z+3^{k}W
with
z\in Z_{N,R}=\{z\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}\colon z+W\subset\Lambda_{N}\}
(these boxes overlap each other significantly by disjoint unions of shifted copies of
W
), shifted to the origin by
z
. (It is of no importance for this proof if we replace
Z_{N,R}
by
\{z\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}\colon z+3^{k}W\subset\Lambda_{N}\}
or not, since we only rely on the limit as
N\to\infty
.)

Up to boundary effects, its
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}
-projection is equal to
\Xi_{N,R}(\omega)
, by which we mean that

\sup_{\omega}{{\rm d}}_{\rm TV}\big(\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}(\omega)),\Xi_{N,R}(\omega)\big)\to 0,\qquad N\to\infty,

(6.11)

where
{{\rm d}}_{\rm TV}
denotes the total-variation distance on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
. Similarly,

\sup_{\omega}{{\rm d}}_{\rm TV}\big(\theta_{z}(\Pi_{z+\widetilde{W}}(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}(\omega))),\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}(\omega))\big)\to 0,\qquad N\to\infty,z\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},\widetilde{W},z+\widetilde{W}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3^{k},W}.

(6.12)

This means that the distance between
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
vanishes as
N\to\infty
, uniformly in
\omega
. By continuity of
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}
, also the distance between
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
and
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
vanishes as
N\to\infty
. Combining with (
6.11
), we see that the distance between
\Xi_{N,W}
and
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
vanishes as
N\to\infty
, uniformly in
\omega
.

As a consequence, for any open set
{\mathcal{U}}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
that entirely lies in the complement of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})=\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
and has a positive distance to it, the probability of
\{\Xi_{N,R}\in{\mathcal{U}}\}
under
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
is zero for all large
N
, since the event is empty. Indeed, the distance of
{\mathcal{U}}
to
\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
is positive, but
\Xi_{N,R}
comes arbitrarily close to it. This implies that the rate function is equal to
+\infty
outside
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Now we use Lemma
6.3
to prove the LDP and to identify the rate function. We conceive the state space
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
as the product
{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\times({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
, which is meant as the set of those pairs
(\mu,(\omega,\varpi))
that are compatible in the sense that
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega,\varpi)=\mu
. That is, the
{\mathcal{L}}
-part of
\xi
does not depend on
\mu
, and
\mu
is the configuration of all the triples of initial sites, lengths and terminal sites of the shreds of
\varpi
. Hence, in Lemma
6.3
,
{\mathcal{X}}={\mathcal{T}}_{W}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}={\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
, and the projection
\pi_{1}
there is called
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
in our situation.

Now consider
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\Lambda_{N}}
, distributed under
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}})
. Conditionally given
(\theta_{z}(\partial\Pi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega)))_{z\in Z_{N,R}}
, the family of pairs
(\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}),\theta_{z}(\omega_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}))_{z\in Z_{N,R}}
has the distribution
\bigotimes_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\zeta_{z}(\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega),\cdot)
, where
\zeta_{z}(\mu,\cdot)
is, for general
\mu
, the product measure of the distribution
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}})
(not depending on
\mu
) and
\mathbb{P}_{\mu}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W_{z},\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
(see Lemma
6.2
(3)). We are under the assumption that the empirical measure of the
\theta_{z}(\partial\Pi_{W_{z}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega))
(namely
\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega}})}}}_{N,R}
) converges towards
\psi
. If
\zeta_{z}(\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega),\cdot)
would not depend on
z
nor on
N
(it does through our boundary condition in
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}})
), i.e., if it would be just equal to
\zeta(\mu,{\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})({\rm d}\omega){\tt K}_{W}(\mu,{\rm d}\varpi)
(see (
2.5
)), then Lemma
6.3
would be directly applicable, and we would obtain that the empirical measure of these pairs (and this is
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\omega}})}}}_{N,R}
) satisfies an LDP with the rate function in (
6.9
). Indeed, the reference measure in (
6.9
) is equal to
\psi\otimes\zeta({\rm d}\mu,{\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))=\psi({\rm d}\mu)\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})({\rm d}\omega){\tt K}_{W}(\mu,{\rm d}\varpi)=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\psi\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))
. Now apply Lemmas
6.2
and
6.3
to finish the proof.
∎

Hence, it is natural to restrict to the set of
\xi
’s and
\psi
’s to those that satisfy
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)=\psi
and to use only the rate function

J_{W}(\xi)=J_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)}})}}}(\xi)=\frac{1}{|W|}H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi\,\big|\,\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]\big),\qquad\xi\in\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}).

(6.13)

Note that
\Pi_{W}(P)
lies automatically in
\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
for
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
, hence for considering
J_{W}(\Pi_{W}(P))
later we will not need to take extra care of the restriction.

Let us formulate Lemma
6.4
in a way in which we will actually apply it later. For the lower bound, because of our approach in Lemmas
4.3
and (
5.5
), we need to condition the integration to the event
\{\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ}})}}}(A_{W;\Theta})=1\}
, where we collect all the cutting parameters in the tuple
\Theta=(M,L,K,\mathfrak{r},T,S,\vartheta)
and summarize the definitions in (
4.34
), (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) and (
5.21
) by putting
A_{W;\Theta}=A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},K,L,T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\times(A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\cap{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}})
. By
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}
we denote probability with respect to the PPP with intensity measure
\nu^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm par}})}}}_{\Lambda_{N},W;\Theta}
, which is defined as the restriction of
\nu_{\Lambda_{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\rm par}}})}}}
defined in (
1.6
) to the event
\bigcap_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\{(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})\in A_{W_{z};\Theta}\}
.

Corollary 6.5

(Upper and lower LDP bounds for
\Xi_{N,R}
)
.

Fix
R\in\mathbb{N}
and
W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}
and a function
F\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})\to\mathbb{R}
and a measurable set
B\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
.

(1)

If
F
is lower semi-continuous and bounded from below and
B
closed and
B^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
compact, then

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}

\displaystyle\Big[{\operatorname{e}}^{-|\Lambda_{N}|F(\Xi_{N,R})}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\Xi_{N,R}\in B\}\{\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in B^{\prime}\}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq-\inf_{\xi\in B\cap\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})\colon\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\in B^{\prime}}\big(F(\xi)+J_{W}(\xi)\big).

(2)

Fix all the parameters
M,L,K,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta,S\in(0,\infty]
and put
\Theta=(M,L,K,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta,S)
. Then, for any
W
-admissible
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
and for any sequence
\psi_{N}\to\psi
such that
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}(\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Xi_{N,R})=\psi_{N})>0
for any
N
, if
F
is upper semi-continuous and
B
is subset of and open in
A_{W;\Theta}
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}

\displaystyle\log{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}\Big[{\operatorname{e}}^{-|\Lambda_{N}|F(\Xi_{N,R})}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\Xi_{N,R}\in B\}\,\Big|\,\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Xi_{N,R})=\psi_{N}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq-\inf_{\xi\in B\colon\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)=\psi}\big(F(\xi)+J_{W;\Theta}(\xi)\big),

where

J_{W;\Theta}(\xi)=\frac{1}{|W|}H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi\,\big|\,\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}_{M})_{L,K}\otimes[\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta,S}})}}}_{W}]\big),\qquad\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}),

(6.14)

where
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}_{M})_{L,K}
is the conditional version of
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}_{M})
given
A_{W;M,L,K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
, and
{\tt K}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\mathfrak{r},T,\vartheta,S}})}}}_{W}(\mu,\cdot)
is the conditional version of
{\tt K}_{W}(\mu,\cdot)
given
A_{W;M,\mathfrak{r},T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
. (In particular,
J_{W;\Theta}(\xi)=\infty
if
\xi(A_{W;\Theta})<1
.)

The upper bound also motivates the following Section
6.3
: we will need good compactness criteria on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
. The lower bound will later be applied for
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
with an ergodic
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
, evoking Lemma
5.4
.

Proof.

(1) follows, by the virtue of the arguments in the proof of Varadhan’s lemma (see
[
DZ10
, Section 4.3]
) from Lemma
6.4
by dropping all probability terms that concern
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Xi_{N,R})
. However, for the application of Varadhan’s lemma, we need to ensure that the rate function
J_{W}
has compact level sets, and this is not true since its reference measure has no autonomous
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
-part. But, by the proof of Lemma
6.8
(1), the intersection of the level set with
\{\xi\colon\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\in B^{\prime}\}
is compact, which suffices for the upper-bound part of Varadhan’s lemma.

To prove (2), again by the virtue of the arguments in the proof of Varadhan’s lemma, we only need to observe that the proof of Lemma
6.4
applies also for the restricted and conditional version
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}_{W;\Theta}
instead of
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
and that
J_{W;\Theta}(\xi)=\infty
if
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\not=\psi
.
∎

6.2.
Properties of
J_{W}

We are going to collect some crucial properties of
J_{W}
defined in (
6.13
), in particular of
J_{W_{R}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))
for shift-invariant distributions
P
of loops and interlacements. These properties will be essential for all the future developments. Our main result here is Proposition
6.6
; it has four assertions.

In the first two assertions, we show that the
J
-value in a box
mW
(with
m\in\mathbb{N}
) of a given configuration measure
\xi
is not smaller than the
J
-value in
W
of a kind of convex combination of parts of
\xi
. This will be used in the proof of Theorem
2.3
when we show that the limit inferior of
J_{W_{R}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))
as
R\to\infty
is large enough for any
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
. The third assertion is a technical help here in form of a certain lower-semicontinuity property. The fourth assertion is in a sense dual to the first one, as it solves the question how to construct from a configuration measure
\xi
in a box
W
a configuration measure
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
in the box
3^{k}W
(in fact, an extension of
\xi
) such that its
J
-values do not significantly increase if
k
gets large. (This will be crucial in the proof of the upper bound in Theorem
2.5
, when we have to make a connection from the variational problem in
W_{R}
to the
\mathbb{R}^{d}
-version of it,
\chi(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
.) Here we construct rather comfortable extensions
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
of
\xi
which lie in the nice set
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
of
W
-shift invariant measures defined in (
6.7
); in particular they are extensions of each other.

The main body of our arguments relies on LDP-arguments instead of the explicit entropy formula in (
6.13
). One of the biggest difficulties comes from the fact that extensions of point measures on shreds are much more complicated than extensions of point measures of loops, and also the latter is not at all clear in our setting, since our
W
-loops have all their particles in
W
: an extension to a larger box
\widetilde{W}
should also produce loops that have particles both in
W
and
\widetilde{W}
. Our LDP-arguments guarantee also that.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
, denote by
Z_{m,R}
the set of those
y\in 2R\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
W_{mR}=mW_{R}
is equal to the pairwise disjoint (up to boundaries) union of all the
y+W_{R}
with
y\in Z_{m,R}
. Furthermore, consider the map

f_{m,R}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{mR}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{mR}})\to{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}),\qquad f_{m}(\xi)=\frac{1}{\#Z_{m,R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{m,R}}\theta_{z}(\Pi_{W_{mR}\to z+W_{R}}(\xi)).

(6.15)

Recall the notion of admissibility from Definition
5.2
and the definitions of
{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
from (
4.30
) and (
4.32
), respectively. Recall the definition of the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
from (
6.7
).

Proposition 6.6

(Estimates for
J_{W}
)
.

Fix
M,R\in(1,\infty)
and any odd
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Recall
\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R},M}
defined in (
5.5
). The constants
C
and
C_{M,\mathfrak{r},T}
stem from Lemma
5.5
, while
C_{M}
appears first in Lemma
5.3
. Then the following hold.

(1)

For any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{mR}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\times{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W_{mR}})
such that
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W_{mR}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
is
W_{mR}
-admissible,

J_{W_{R};M}(f_{m,R}(\xi))\leq J_{W_{mR};M}(\xi)+\frac{C_{M}}{(mR)^{d/2}}-\frac{1}{|W_{mR}|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W_{mR},M}\rangle,

(6.16)

and, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
x+W_{R}\subset W_{mR}
,

J_{W_{R};M}(\theta_{x}(\Pi_{x+W_{R}}(\xi)))\leq J_{W_{mR};M}(\xi)+\frac{C_{M}}{(mR)^{d/2}}-\frac{1}{|W_{mR}|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W_{mR},M}\rangle.

(6.17)

where
J_{W;M}
is defined in (
6.14
) with all other parameters
K,L,T,\vartheta
equal to
\infty
.

(2)

For any
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\times{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
such that
P
is
W_{mR}
-ergodic and
\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{R}}(P)
is concentrated on
A^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W_{R};M,\mathfrak{r},T}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\vartheta,S}})}}}
,

\displaystyle J_{W_{R};M}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))

\displaystyle\leq J_{W_{mR};M}(\Pi_{W_{mR}}(P))+\frac{C_{M}}{(mR)^{d/2}}+\Big[\frac{C_{M,\mathfrak{r},T}}{mR}+\frac{C\vartheta^{2}}{S}\Big]\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\rangle.

(6.18)

(3)

For any
P,P_{1},P_{2},\dots\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
such that
P_{n}
converges weakly to
P
, we have
J_{W_{R}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))\leq\liminf_{n\to\infty}J_{W_{R_{n}}}(\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}(P_{n}))
for any sequence
R_{n}\downarrow R
.

(4)

For any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W_{R}})
such that
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
is
W_{R}
-admissible, and for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle J_{W_{R};M}(\xi)

\displaystyle\geq\inf\big\{J_{3^{k}W_{R};M}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})\colon\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}},\Pi_{3^{k}W_{R}\to W_{R}}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})=\xi\big\}-\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}}+\frac{1}{|W_{R}|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R},M}\rangle.

(6.19)

Proof.

Recall that
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}
denotes the PPP with intensity measure given in (
4.31
). All asymptotic assertions as
N\to\infty
in the following are meant under this measure.

(1) Note that
f_{m,R}(\Xi_{N,mR})=\Xi_{N,R}
for any large
N\in\mathbb{N}
that is an odd multiple of
mR
. Hence, for any
\delta
-neighbourhood
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}
of
\xi
, there is an
\delta^{\prime}
-neighbourhood
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}
of
f_{m,R}(\xi)
such that

{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,mR}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}\big)\leq{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,R}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}\big).

(6.20)

We take
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}
such that
\widetilde{U}=\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}})=\{\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})\colon\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}\}
is compact. Then, according to Lemma
6.4
(or Corollary
6.5
(1)), the right-hand side can be further estimated as follows.

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,R}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}\big)\leq-\inf_{\psi\in\widetilde{U}}\inf_{\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}}J_{W_{R};M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\psi}})}}}(\xi^{\prime})=-\inf_{\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}}J_{W_{R};M}(\xi^{\prime}),

where the last step derives from the fact that
J_{W_{R};M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\psi}})}}}(\xi^{\prime})=\infty
if
\psi\not=\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi^{\prime})
. Because of the lower semicontinuity of
J_{W_{R};M}
, we can estimate
-\inf_{\xi^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}}J_{W_{R};M}(\xi^{\prime})\leq-J_{W_{R};M}(f_{m,R}(\xi))+C_{\delta}
for some
C_{\delta}
that vanishes as
\delta\downarrow 0
; here we use that
\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}
is an
\delta^{\prime}
-neighbourhood of
f_{m,R}(\xi)
, and
\delta^{\prime}
vanishes as
\delta\downarrow 0
.

The left-hand side of (
6.20
) is lower estimated using a similar strategy to what we will present in Section
6.5
. Indeed, we can lower bound

{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,mR}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}\big)\geq{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,mR}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}\,\big|\,\partial\Xi_{N,mR}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=\psi_{N}\big){\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\partial\Xi_{N,mR}=\psi_{N}),

where
\psi_{N}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}})
are picked such that they converge towards
\psi
as
N\to\infty
. Now Lemma
5.3
and Corollary
6.5
(2) give that

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}

\displaystyle\log{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,mR}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}\big)\geq-\inf_{\widetilde{\xi}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}\colon\partial\Pi_{W_{mR}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\widetilde{\xi})=\psi}J_{W_{mR};M}(\widetilde{\xi})

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{M}}{(mR)^{d/2}}+\frac{1}{|W_{mR}|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W_{mR},M}\rangle,

recalling that
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W_{mR}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(f_{m,R}(\xi))
is
W_{mR}
-admissible.
Clearly, the infimum on the right-hand side is not larger than
J_{W_{mR};M}(\xi)
. This yields

\displaystyle-J_{W_{R};M}(f_{m,R}(\xi))

\displaystyle+C_{\delta}\geq-J_{W_{mR};M}(\xi)-\frac{C_{M}}{(mR)^{d/2}}+\frac{1}{|W_{mR}|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W_{mR},M}\rangle.

(6.21)

Making
\delta\downarrow 0
, this implies (
6.16
).

For proving (
6.17
), fix
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
x+W_{R}\subset W_{mR}
and an odd
m\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\{1\}
and define

f^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{mR}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{mR}})\to{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}),\qquad f^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({m}})}}})=\theta_{x}\big(\Pi_{x+W_{R}}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({m}})}}})\big).

(6.22)

Then
f^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}(\Xi_{N,mR}(\omega))=\Xi_{N,mR}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}(\omega)=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,mR}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,mR}}\delta_{\theta_{z+x}(\Pi_{z+x+W_{R}}(\omega))}
. We proceed now as above with
f_{m,R}
replaced by
f^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}
. Indeed, for any
\delta
-neighbourhood
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}
of
\Pi_{W_{mR}}(P)
, there is an
\delta^{\prime}
-neighbourhood
{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}
of
f^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}(\Pi_{W_{mR}}(P))=\theta_{x}(\Pi_{x+W_{R}}(P))
such that

{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,mR}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}\big)\leq{\tt Q}_{M}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}_{N,mR}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}_{\delta^{\prime}}\big).

(6.23)

For further estimating the right-hand side, observe that Lemma
6.4
applies to
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}_{N,mR}
when conditioned on
\{\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}_{N,mR}=\psi_{N}\}
for some
\psi_{N}\to\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}})
, where we define
\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({x}})}}}_{N,mR}=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,mR}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,mR}}\delta_{\theta_{z+x}(\partial\Pi_{z+x+W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega))}
, since both
{\tt Q}_{M}
and the kernel
{\tt K}_{W}
are shift-invariant and can be equally applied in
x+W_{R}
as in
W_{R}
. Therefore, as above, we arrive at (
6.17
).

(2) We apply (1) to
\xi=\Pi_{W_{mR}}(P)
and note that
f_{m,R}(\Pi_{W_{mR}}(P))=\Pi_{W_{R}}(P)
by stationarity of
P
. Note that
\partial\Pi_{W_{mR}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
is
W_{mR}
-admissible by Lemma
5.4
. Use Lemma
5.5
for
mR
instead of
R
in order to estimate the right-hand side of (
6.16
).

(3) Fix
R\in(0,\infty)
and a sequence
(R_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
that tends to
R
from above. Using (
2.7
), we see that, for any bounded measurable function
f\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R_{n}}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R_{n}}}\to\mathbb{R}
,

|W_{R_{n}}|J_{W_{R_{n}}}(\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}(P))\geq\langle\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}(P),f\rangle-\log\big[\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W_{R_{n}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)\otimes{\tt K}_{W_{R_{n}}}\big]({\operatorname{e}}^{f}).

We use this for a function
f
that depends only on
{\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}
, i.e., for an
f
of the form
f=g\circ\Pi_{W_{R}}
with some bounded measurable
g\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}\to\mathbb{R}
. Using that
R\leq R_{n}
and hence
\Pi_{W_{R}}\circ\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}=\Pi_{W_{R}}
, we see that

\langle\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}(P),f\rangle=\langle P,f\circ\Pi_{W_{R_{n}}}\rangle=\langle P,g\circ\Pi_{W_{R}}\rangle=\langle\Pi_{W_{R}}(P),g\rangle.

On the other hand, for such an
f
, the integral of
{\operatorname{e}}^{f}
with respect to
\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W_{R_{n}}}({\tt Q})
is equal to the integral of
{\operatorname{e}}^{g}
with respect to
\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W_{R}}({\tt Q})
, and the integral of
{\operatorname{e}}^{g}
with respect to
\partial\Pi_{W_{n}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P_{n})\otimes{\tt K}_{W_{R_{n}}}
is asymptotic to its integral with respect to
\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)\otimes{\tt K}_{W_{R}}
as
n\to\infty
, since the
P_{n}
-probability that a
W_{R_{n}}
-shred is not a
W_{R}
-shred vanishes. Picking
g
such that the supremum over all bounded measurable functions
{\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}\to\mathbb{R}
is approached in the formula (
2.7
) for
|W_{R}|J_{W_{R}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}(P))
, we arrive at the assertion.

(4) Recall the empirical process
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
defined in (
6.10
) and recall from (
6.11
) that
\Xi_{N,R}
and
\Pi_{W}(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
have a vanishing total-variation distance as
N\to\infty
, where we abbreviate here and in the rest of the proof
\Pi_{W}=\Pi_{3^{k}W\to W}
and
W=W_{R}
. We also note that

\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}=\frac{1}{\#(3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d})}\sum_{y\in 3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}},\qquad\mbox{where }\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}}=\frac{1}{\#Z_{N,3^{k}R}}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,3^{k}R}}\delta_{\theta_{y+z}(\Pi_{y+z+3^{k}W}(\omega))},

(6.24)

and note, for any
y\in 3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, that
(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
can be shown to be exponentially equivalent to
(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,0}})}}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}=(\Xi_{N,3^{k}R})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, since they are only a fixed shift of each other, which corresponds to replacing
\Lambda_{N}
by
\Lambda_{N}+y
. In particular, every
(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies, according to (a variant of) Corollary
6.5
, the LDP upper bound with rate function
J_{3^{k}W;M}
.

We introduce the projection
\pi_{y}\colon{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W})^{3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
on the
y
-th factor. We first show that
((\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}})_{y})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies the LDP upper bound with rate function
(\xi_{y})_{y}\mapsto\max_{y}J_{3^{k}W}(\xi_{y})
. Indeed, for any closed set
U
in

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W})^{3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
, we can easily estimate, for any
y
,

{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}\big((\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}})_{y}\in U\big)\leq{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}_{M}\big(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}}\in\pi_{y}(U)\big)\leq\exp\Big\{-(1+o(1))|\Lambda_{N}|\inf_{\pi_{y}(U)}J_{3^{k}W,M}\Big\}.

We abbreviate
G((\xi_{y})_{y\in 3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}})=\frac{1}{\#(3^{k}W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d})}\sum_{y}\xi_{y}
, then
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}=G((\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,y}})}}})_{y})
satisfies, according to the (upper-bound half of the) contraction principle from the theory of large deviations (see
[
DZ10
, Section 4.2.1]
) the upper-bound half of the LDP with rate function
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{3^{k}W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{3^{k}W})\ni\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\mapsto\inf\{\max_{y}J_{3^{k}W,M}\circ\pi_{y}((\xi_{y})_{y})\colon G((\xi_{y})_{y})=\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\}
. Since
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
has a vanishing distance to the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
, the rate function is
=\infty
in points
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\in(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})^{\rm c}
. Note that this rate function is not smaller than
J_{3^{k}W,M}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
, since, by convexity,
\max_{y}J_{3^{k}W,M}\circ\pi_{y}((\xi_{y^{\prime}})_{y^{\prime}})\geq G((J_{3^{k}W,M}(\xi_{y})_{y})\geq J_{3^{k}W,M}(G((\xi_{y}))_{y})=J_{3^{k}W,M}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
. Hence,
\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
satisfies the upper-bound half of the LDP with rate function
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\mapsto J_{3^{k}W,M}(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
if
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
and
\mapsto\infty
otherwise. Now recall that
\Xi_{N,R}
(and therefore
\Pi_{W}(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}})
, since they have vanishing total-variation distance) satisfies the LDP lower bound with rate function
\xi\mapsto J_{W,M}(\xi)+\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}}-\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\psi,\mathfrak{p}_{W,M}\rangle
(for a proof, adapt the arguments of the proof of (1)). Again using the contraction principle for the function
\Pi_{W}
, we see that (
6.19
) is satisfied.
∎

6.3.
Compactnesses

In Section
6.4
we will apply the LDP of Corollary
6.5
(1) to the right-hand side of (
4.25
). For this, it is necessary to generate compactness in the integration area beforehand. Furthermore, we need to introduce a condition that makes the two particle densities that appear in (
4.25
) continuous functions of
\Xi_{N,R}
, since it is crucial for our purposes to keep control on the condensate density and the total density of particles. The purpose of this section is to introduce this condition and to show how it can be incorporated in (
4.25
).

Part of our solution to this problem is to prove the compactness of the sub-level sets of
J_{W}
, which will be also used in the proof of Theorem
2.3
. But we will also need to explicitly control the expected particle number of the boundary-shred configuration
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
, since this is not controlled in the sub-level sets.

More explicitly, we will apply the (upper bound of the) LDP on the event
\{\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{N,R}\in K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\}\cap\{\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}\in K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\}
, where
K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
and
K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
are sets that need to satisfy three crucial properties:

(1)

they are closed, and
K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
induces compactness for the
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
-part on the sub-level sets of
J_{W}
,

(2)

the probability of
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{N,R}
, respectively
\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}
, being in their complement is extremely small on the exponential scale,

(3)

on these sets, the number of particles is a continuous function of
\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{N,R}
respectively
\partial\Xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{N,R}
.

The problem with (3) is that,
a priori
, the maps
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle
, respectively
\psi\mapsto\langle\psi,\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
, are not continuous in the weak topology since neither
\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
nor
\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
are bounded.
Therefore, we make use of Assumption (V), in particular (
2.23
), which gives an efficient bound on having an extraordinarily high number of particles in the subboxes. Hence, (2) will be handled in terms of the transformed probability measure
\widehat{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm bc}}})}}}
defined in (
1.13
).

6.3.1.
Loop part.

Following Remark
2.11
, we decided to work (instead with the number
{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
of particles in all loops in
W_{R}
; see (
2.15
)) with the number
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega)
of particles in
y+U
(recall that
U=[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}=W_{1/2}
) coming from any of the loops of
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}_{W}
starting in
W
. Note that
\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega)={\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega)
, where
Z_{R,1/2}
(in a small abuse of notation) is the set of those
y\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
y+U\subset W_{R}
(which implies
|Z_{R,1/2}|\leq|W_{R}|
). Then we consider the set

\displaystyle K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}})\colon\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\big\langle\xi,\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\big)^{5/4}\big\rangle\leq L|W_{R}|\Big\},\qquad L\in(0,\infty).

(6.25)

In words, under any
\xi\in K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
, the number of particles in any shift of
U
in
W_{R}
is not extremely unevenly distributed, uniformly over the subbox and over
\xi
.

The set
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
turns out to possess the mentioned three properties:

Lemma 6.7

(Restriction of the loop-part)
.

Suppose that Assumption (V) holds, then the following assertions hold.

(1)

For any
L\in(0,\infty)
and for any
R\in\mathbb{N}
, the set
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
is closed in the weak topology.

(2)

\lim_{L\to\infty}\sup_{R\in\mathbb{N}}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log\widehat{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\Big(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\in\big(K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)\big)^{\rm c}\Big)=-\infty.

(6.26)

(3)

For any
L\in(0,\infty)
and for any
R\in\mathbb{N}
, the map
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle
is continuous on the set
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
.

Proof.

We drop the super-indices ‘
{\mathcal{L}}
’, and we abbreviate
W=W_{R}
.

(1) Since the map
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{U}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{U}>L_{k}\}\rangle
is lower semi-continuous for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
K_{R}(L)
is closed.

(2) Now we show (
6.26
). We adapt and extend the proof of Lemma
3.2
and will also employ Lemma
3.1
(which uses (
2.23
) from Assumption (V)). As always, we assume that
\Lambda_{N}
is equal to the (up to boundaries) disjoint union of the boxes
W_{z}=z+W
with
z\in Z_{N,R}
(and
R
may depend on
N
). We use that
\#Z_{N,R}\sim|\Lambda_{N}|/|W|
as
N\to\infty
.

First note that, for any
y\in Z_{R,1/2}
, in the limit as
N\to\infty
,

\big\langle\Xi_{N,R}(\omega_{\rm P}),\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{y+U}\big)^{5/4}\big\rangle\sim\frac{|W|}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U}(\omega_{\rm P})\big)^{5/4}.

Hence, for any
L\in(0,\infty)
, recalling the notation in (
4.26
),

\displaystyle\widehat{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}

\displaystyle\big(\Xi_{N,R}\in(K_{R}(L))^{\rm c}\big)\leq\widehat{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\Big(\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U}(\omega_{W_{z}})\big)^{5/4}>L|\Lambda_{N}|\Big)

(6.27)

\displaystyle\leq{\operatorname{e}}^{-L|\Lambda_{N}|}\widehat{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\Big({\operatorname{e}}^{\sum_{z\in Z_{N,R}}\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U}(\omega_{W_{z}})\big)^{5/4}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\operatorname{e}}^{-L|\Lambda_{N}|}}{\widehat{Z}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}_{N}(\Lambda_{N})}\prod_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\Big({\operatorname{e}}^{\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U}(\omega_{W_{z}})\big)^{5/4}}{\operatorname{e}}^{-\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\Phi_{z+y+U,z+y+U}(\omega_{W_{z}})}\Big),

where we used that, under particle boundary condition, the configurations in the subboxes
W_{z}
are i.i.d. over
z
, and we estimated the total interaction
\Phi_{\Lambda_{n},\Lambda_{N}}
in
\Lambda_{N}
from below against the sum of the interactions within the
z+y+U
(i.e., the interactions between any pair of different legs that start in
z+y+U
).

We apply Lemma
3.1
for any
z
and
y
to the expectation of
{\operatorname{e}}^{-\Phi_{z+y+U,z+y+U}}
over all the legs starting in
z+y+U
, conditioned on their starting sites, which are the particles. Hence, there is some
C\in(0,\infty)
such that this conditional expectation is upper bounded by
{\operatorname{e}}^{-C(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U})^{3/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U}\geq C\}}
. Abbreviate
f(m)=m^{5/4}-Cm^{3/2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{m\geq C\}
. Hence, when carrying out the expectation over all the (independent!) legs in the configuration in each subbox
z+y+U
, we obtain from (
6.27
) the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\widehat{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\big(\Xi_{N,R}\in(K_{R}(L))^{\rm c}\big)\leq\frac{{\operatorname{e}}^{-L|\Lambda_{N}|}}{\widehat{Z}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}_{N}(\Lambda_{N})}\prod_{z\in Z_{N,R}}{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\Big[{\operatorname{e}}^{\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}f(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+y+U}(\omega_{W_{z}}))}\Big].

(6.28)

Since
f
is bounded from above (here is the point where we need the decomposition into the sum over
y
of the
5/4
-moments of the particle numbers in the unit boxes in the definition of
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
instead of directly bounding only the
5/4
-moment of the particle number in
W
), and since the number of summands
(z,y)
does not depend on
R
and is
\sim|\Lambda_{N}|
, and noting that the normalization constant
\widehat{Z}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}_{N}(\Lambda_{N})
has a positive exponential rate, this shows that (
6.26
) holds.

(3) First note that the map
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\rangle
is lower semicontinuous on
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
, since
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
can be monotonously approached from below with nonnegative bounded continuous functions. Furthermore, the upper semicontinuity can be easily be derived, since on
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
there is a uniform upper bound for a higher moment of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
. More precisely, for any sequence
(\xi_{n})_{n}
in
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)
, tending weakly towards some
\xi\in K_{R}(L)
, we use that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}=\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
and estimate, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
T\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\big|\langle\xi_{n}-\xi,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\rangle\big|\leq\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\Big(\big|\langle\xi_{n}-\xi,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\leq T\}\rangle\big|+T^{-1/4}\langle\xi_{n}+\xi,(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}})^{5/4}\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\big|\langle\xi_{n}-\xi,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\leq T\}\rangle\big|+T^{-1/4}L|W|.

The latter term can be made arbitrarily small by picking
T
large, and then the former sum can be made arbitrarily small by picking
n
large.
∎

6.3.2.
Compactness for shred part.

Analogously to the loop part, we present now a strategy to compactify the boundary-shred-part in the variational formula in (
4.25
) such that the number of particles in the shreds, i.e., the map
\psi\mapsto\langle\psi,{\mathfrak{N}}_{\partial W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
, becomes continuous, where we recall from (
4.16
) that
{\mathfrak{N}}_{\partial W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\mu)=\sum_{i\in I}l_{i}
denotes the number of particles in the configuration
\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
. We also denote by
{\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\varpi)
the number of particles in
y+U
from all the shreds in
\varpi\in{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
(compare to (
2.15
)).

Then we consider the set

\displaystyle K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L)

\displaystyle=\Big\{\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}})\colon\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\big\langle\xi,\big({\mathfrak{N}}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\big)^{5/4}\big\rangle\leq L|W_{R}|\Big\},\qquad L\in(0,\infty).

(6.29)

The set
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
turns out to possess the mentioned three properties:

Lemma 6.8

(Compactification of the shred-part)
.

Suppose that Assumption (V) holds. Then the following holds.

(1)

For any
R,L\in\mathbb{N}
, the set
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L)
is closed in the weak topology.
Furthermore, the set

\big\{\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}})\colon\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\in K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L),\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi),\mathfrak{N}_{\partial{W_{R}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle=\rho_{2},J_{W_{R}}(\xi)\leq C\big\}

(6.30)

is compact for any
\rho_{2},C\in(0,\infty)
.

(2)

\lim_{L\to\infty}\sup_{R\in\mathbb{N}}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log\widehat{{\tt Q}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\Lambda_{N},{\rm par}}})}}}\Big(\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in\big(K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L)\big)^{\rm c}\Big)=-\infty.

(6.31)

(3)

For any
R,L\in\mathbb{N}
, the map
\psi\mapsto\int\psi({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i\in I}l_{i}
is continuous on
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L)
.

Proof.

Much of this proof is analogous to the one of Lemma
6.7
, and we leave the details to the reader. Indeed, the closedness of
K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L)
is analogous, the proof of (2) and the one of (3) as well. However, the compactness of the set in (
6.30
) needs to be shown. Here we can restrict to showing the relative compactness of the set of
\xi
such that
J_{W_{R}}(\xi)\leq C
and
\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi),\mathfrak{N}_{\partial{W_{R}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle\leq\rho_{2}
, since the continuity of the map
\xi\mapsto\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi),\mathfrak{N}_{\partial{W_{R}}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
follows from assertion (3).

First recall from the end of Section
4.2
that the set
K_{\rho_{2}}
of all
\psi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}})
such that
\langle\psi,{\mathfrak{N}}_{\partial W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle\leq\rho_{2}
is relatively compact. Hence, we only need to show that its intersection with the sub-level set of
J_{W_{R}}
(more precisely, the set of
\xi
such that
\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
is in that set and
J_{W_{R}}(\xi)\leq C
) is relative compact in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W_{R}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}})
.

Pick a sequence
(\xi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in that set. Using the standard Lemma
8.2
below we see that its loop-part, i.e., the sequence
(H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}(\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{n})\mid\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, is bounded. Therefore, as it is proved in the large-deviations literature as part of the proof of Sanov’s theorem,
(\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight. Therefore, we only need to show that the shred-part
(\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is also tight. Then
(\Pi_{W_{R}}(\xi_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is also tight, since, for any compact sets
K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{W}
and
K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{W}
, also
K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\times K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
is compact, and its complement is contained in
[(K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}})^{\rm c}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}]\cup[{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times(K^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}})^{\rm c}]
.

Let us show tightness of the sequence
(\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Again from (
2.16
) and Lemma
8.2
we see that it lies in the set

A_{R}=\big\{\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}})\colon\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\in K_{\rho_{2}},H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}}(\xi\mid\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W_{R}})\leq C_{R}\big\},

(6.32)

for some
C_{R}\in\mathbb{R}
.

We show now that this set is compact. The following is an adaptation of the standard proof of compactness of level sets of entropies, as it can be found in the literature on Sanov’s theorem (see
[
DZ10
, Section 6.2]
). Indeed, let a small
\delta\in(0,1)
be given. Since
K_{\rho_{2}}
is compact, there is a compact set
L_{1}\subset{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}}
such that
\psi(L_{1}^{\rm c})\leq\delta
for any
\psi\in K_{R}
. Note that there is an
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
such that, for any
\mu=\sum_{i}\delta_{(x_{i},y_{i},l_{i})}\in L_{1}
, we have
|y_{i}|\leq\alpha
,
l_{i}\leq\alpha
and
|\mu|\leq\alpha
. Additionally, let a small
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
be given. Then, for any
\mu\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}}
, there is a compact set
L_{2}(\mu)\subset{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}
such that
{\tt K}_{W_{R}}(\mu,L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c})\leq\varepsilon
. Then the set
L=\bigcup_{\mu\in L_{1}}\{\mu\}\times L_{2}(\mu)
is compact.

We denote a regular version of the conditional distribution of
\xi
given
\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
by
\xi(\mu,\cdot)
(in earlier notation
\xi_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}}(\mu,\cdot)
). Using the characterisation of the entropy from (
2.7
), we see by taking
f={\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c}}\log M
for any large
M
that, for any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}})
,

H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}}(\xi(\mu,\cdot)\mid{\tt K}_{W_{R}}(\mu,\cdot))\geq\xi(\mu,L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c})\log M-\log\big(1-{\tt K}_{W_{R}}(\mu,L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c})+M{\tt K}_{W_{R}}(\mu,L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c})\big),

and hence

\xi(\mu,L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c})\leq\frac{1}{\log M}\Big[H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}}(\xi(\mu,\cdot)\mid{\tt K}_{W_{R}}(\mu,\cdot))+\log(1+(M-1)\varepsilon)\Big].

Now we assume that
\xi\in A_{R}
and integrate over
\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)({\rm d}\mu)
to obtain

\displaystyle\xi\Big(\bigcup_{\mu\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W_{R}}}\{\mu\}\times L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c}\Big)

\displaystyle=\int\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)({\rm d}\mu)\,\xi(\mu,L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log M}H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W_{R}}}(\xi\mid\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W_{R}})+\frac{\log(1+(M-1)\varepsilon)}{\log M}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c|W_{R}|+\log(1+(M-1)\varepsilon)}{\log M}.

Now picking
M=1+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}
and making
\varepsilon
small, we can press the right-hand side under the given small number
\delta\in(0,1)
. Now we want to argue that
\xi(L^{\rm c})\leq 2\delta
for any
\xi\in A_{R}
.

Indeed, split
L^{\rm c}
into the union of
\{\mu\}\times L_{2}(\mu)^{\rm c}
with
\mu\in L_{1}
and the union of
\{\mu\}\times L_{2}(\mu)
with
\mu\in L_{1}^{\rm c}
to see that the
\xi
-measure of the complement of the former is below
\delta
by the choice of
L_{1}
and the one of the latter is below
\delta
by the preceding argument. This finishes the proof of tightness of
A_{R}
and therefore the proof of the entire lemma.
∎

6.4.
Upper large deviations bound

In this section we derive an upper bound for the large-
N
exponential rate of
\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
defined in (
4.24
), on the base of the upper bound of Lemma
4.2
in (
4.25
). Recall that we fixed
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in(0,\infty)
with
\rho=\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}
. Furthermore, as always, we fix
R\in\mathbb{N}
and decompose the box
\Lambda_{N}
into the subboxes
z+W
with
z\in Z_{N,R}
, where
