R
might depend on
N
and converges then to
R
. Furthermore, we fix a small
\delta\in(0,1)
.

We want to apply Corollary
6.5
(1) to the right-hand side of (
4.25
). For this, we insert the indicator on
\{\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\in K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L)\}\cap\{\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L)\}
, plus the one on its complement, and we note that the second part (the one with the indicator on the complement) is negligible on the exponential scale in the limit
N\to\infty
by Lemmas
6.7
and
6.8
, if
L
is taken large enough (not depending on
R
, according to assertion (2) there). On the first event, the map
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi,\frac{1}{|W|}F_{W,W}\rangle
is lower semicontinuous, and the two maps
\xi\mapsto\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle
and
\psi\mapsto\langle\psi,\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
are continuous; hence the two indicators on the right-hand side of (
4.25
) are on closed sets, and the
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
-part is even continuous. Applying Corollary
6.5
(1), we arrive at

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}

\displaystyle\log\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})

(6.33)

\displaystyle\leq-\inf\Big\{\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\xi,F_{W,W}\rangle+J_{W}(\xi)\colon\xi\in\bigcap_{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}),

\displaystyle\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi)\in K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(L),\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\in K_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(L),

\displaystyle\langle\xi,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|{W}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}_{2\delta}(\rho_{1}),\langle\xi,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|{W}|}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle\in\overline{{\mathcal{B}}}_{2\delta}(\rho_{2})\Big\}.

In Section
8
we show how to handle the limit as
R\to\infty
for the crucial entropy term
J_{W}(\xi)
if
\xi=\Pi_{W}(P)
for some
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
. The finish of the proof of the upper bound in Theorem
2.5
will be carried out in Section
9.1
.

6.5.
Lower large deviations bound

Now we apply the large deviations assertions of Corollary
6.5
(2) to our preliminary lower bound in Lemma
4.3
in (
4.39
) to obtain a lower bound for the limiting free energy. We keep
R\in\mathbb{N}
fixed and put
W=[-R,R]^{d}
. Furthermore, we fix also all the auxiliary parameters
\mathfrak{r},M,K,L,T\in(0,\infty)
and recall the events in (
4.34
), (
4.35
) and (
4.36
). We also recall that
\Lambda_{N}^{\prime}
is the ‘interior’ of
\Lambda_{N}
, i.e., the union of the
W_{z}
with
z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}
.
Recall also other notation from Section
4.6
, in particular that we write
\|f\|_{{\rm sp}}=\max_{t\in[0,\beta]}|f(t)-f(0)|
for the spread of a leg
f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
. We pick any admissible
\psi^{*}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{T}}_{W})
(see Definition
5.2
). We assume that
\langle\psi^{*},\frac{1}{|W|}\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{2})
. For some small
\delta\in(0,1)
, we insert the indicator on the event
\{\partial\Xi_{N,R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circ,\omega_{\rm P},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\psi^{*})\}
into the expectation on the right-hand side of (
4.39
), where
{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta}(\psi^{*})
is a
\delta
-neighbourhood of
\psi^{*}
. Recall the kernel
{\tt K}_{W}
from (
2.5
).
We are going to use
C\in(0,\infty)
as a generic constant that depends only on
d
,
\beta
,
\rho_{1}
,
\rho_{2}
or
v
and may change its value from appearance to appearance.

In view of an application of Corollary
6.5
(2) we switched already in (
4.39
) in Section
4.6
to the integration with respect the conditional version given an event, namely
\bigcap_{z\in Z_{N,R}^{\circ}}\{(\omega_{W_{z}},\varpi_{W_{z}})\in A_{W_{z};M,L,K,T}\}
, on which the particle numbers in loops and in shreds, respectively, and the interaction are continuous functionals of
\Xi_{N,R}
. This is necessary in order that the event

B=\Big\{\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}(A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,K,L,T})\colon\langle\xi,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{1})\mbox{ and }\langle\xi,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{2})\Big\}

is open in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}(A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,K,L,T})
and the map
\xi\mapsto\langle F_{W,W},\xi\rangle
is continuous. Indeed the functionals
\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
,
\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
and
F_{W,W}
need to be bounded and continuous on our restriction, since we are working in the weak topology. Note that their continuity is not a problem, since (see Section
4.1
) convergence of loop-shred configurations is defined leg-wise, and
V
defined in (
1.10
) is continuous. Indeed, on
A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}
, the three functions
\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
,
\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
and
F_{W,W}
are bounded. This is clear for the first two, and for
F_{W,W}
this follows from Lemma
4.4
with
z=z^{\prime}
. Hence, we are in the setting of our crucial LDP-tool, Corollary
6.5
(2).

Now taking the limit
N\to\infty
in (
4.39
) gives, according to Lemma
5.3
and Corollary
6.5
(2),

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\geq-\frac{C_{M,L,K,T}}{R}-\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}}+\langle\psi^{*},\mathfrak{p}_{W_{R},M}\rangle

(6.34)

\displaystyle-\inf\Big\{\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\xi,F_{W,W}\rangle+J_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}(\xi)-\langle\log p_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T},\psi^{*}\rangle\colon

\displaystyle\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}(A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}),\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)=\psi^{*},\langle\xi,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{1})\Big\},

where
J_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}
is defined in (
6.14
) with
\vartheta=\infty
. Note that, for
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
that is concentrated on
A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}
with
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)=\psi^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}(\xi)-\langle\log p_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T},\psi^{*}\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}}\xi({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))\,\log\frac{{\rm d}\xi}{{\rm d}[\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\psi^{*}\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]]}(\omega,\varpi)

\displaystyle=J_{W}(\xi),

since the integral on
A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}^{\rm c}
does not contribute. We take
\psi^{*}
of this form.

In order to apply Lemma
5.5
to the third term on the right-hand side of (
6.34
), we additionally require that
\xi
is concentrated on
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
where
{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
is defined in (
5.21
). We further confine the infimum to those
\xi
’s of the form
\xi=\Pi_{W}(P)
with an ergodic measure
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
. Then
\psi^{*}=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
is admissible according to Lemma
5.4
. This implies that, for any
R,M,L,K,T,\vartheta,S,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\Lambda_{N}|}\log\widehat{Z}_{N,R,\delta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm bc}})}}}(\Lambda_{N},\rho_{1},\rho_{2})\geq-\frac{C_{M,L,K,T}}{R}-\frac{C_{M}}{R^{d/2}}-\Big[\frac{C_{M,\vartheta,S}}{R}+\frac{C\vartheta^{2}}{S}\Big](\rho_{2}+\delta^{\prime})

(6.35)

\displaystyle-\inf\Big\{\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\Pi_{W}(P),F_{W,W}\rangle+J_{W}(\Pi_{W}(P))\colon P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})\mbox{ ergodic, }

\displaystyle\Pi_{W}(P)(A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T})=1=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)({\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}),\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta/3}(\rho_{1}),\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\delta^{\prime}}(\rho_{2})\Big\}.

In Lemma
7.6
we will show how to get rid of the two restrictions of
P
being ergodic and
\Pi_{W}(P)
being concentrated on
A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}
. In Section
9.2
, we put this all together, make
R\to\infty
and afterwards
M,T\to\infty
and then
L,K\to\infty
and finish the proof of the lower bound in Theorem
2.5
.

7.
Analysis of the variational formula

This section is devoted to proofs of a number of results that evolve around the variational formula
\chi(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
in (
2.24
).
In Section
7.1
we give the proof of Lemma
2.12
from Section
2.4
, the regularity properties of
\chi(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
. The other sections give a number of technical preparations for some crucial points in the proofs of Theorems
2.3
and
2.5
. That is, in Section
7.2
we explicitly decompose the entropies for loop point processes and for interlacement point processes according to the various parts of these point processes. In Section
7.3
we use this to prove Lemma
2.13
, i.e., that every loop/interlacement configuration can be approximated with a pure loop-configuration. In Section
7.4
we show that entropies of loop-shred configuration measures that are concentrated on uniformly bounded configurations approximate the entropy of arbitrary loop-shred configurations if the bound diverges. Using this and a similar technique as in Section
7.3
, in Section
7.5
we carry out an ergodic approximation in our main variational formula, i.e., we prove that the minimizers can be approximated by ergodic loop/interlacements configurations that are additionally uniformly bounded in various senses.

7.1.
Proof of Lemma
2.12

In this section, we prove Lemma
2.12
.
As a preparation, we give a crucial lower bound on the expected energy
F_{U}
defined in (
2.22
), which will be important for obtaining compactness properties. By
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega,\varpi)
we denote the number of particles in
W
in the loop/interlacement configuration
(\omega,\varpi)
, regardless where the loop starts in which is the particle is contained (if any). In Remark
2.11
, we argued that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
has the same expectation under any
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
as
{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
defined in and below (
2.15
).

Lemma 7.1

(Lower bound for expected interaction)
.

Suppose that
v
satisfies Assumption (V). Then, for any ergodic
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
,

\langle P,F_{U}\rangle\geq\frac{C\beta}{2}\big\langle P,\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\big)^{2}\big\rangle-\frac{5C\beta}{4},

(7.1)

where
C
is introduced in (
2.23
). Moreover, the maps
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})\ni P\mapsto\langle P,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})\ni P\mapsto\langle P,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
are continuous on the set
\{P\colon\langle P,(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}})^{2}\rangle\leq L\}
for any
L\in(0,\infty)
.

Proof.

According to Remark
2.11
, we can replace
\langle P,F_{U}\rangle
by the expectation of
\widetilde{F}_{U}(\omega,\varpi)
, the amount of interaction in the loop/interlacement configuration between any leg that starts in
U
(regardless, whether it is in a loop or in an interlacement) and any other leg. Define
\widetilde{F}_{W}
in the same way with
U
replaced by
W=[-R,R]^{d}
. Define
\mathfrak{L}(\omega,\varpi)
as the set of all legs of
\omega
or of
\varpi
. Then, by shift-invariance, for
R\in\mathbb{N}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\langle P,F_{U}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|W|}\langle P,\widetilde{F}_{W}\rangle\geq\frac{1}{|W|}\int_{0}^{\beta}{\rm d}s\,\int P({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))\,\sum_{g,g^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{L}(\omega,\varpi)\colon g(s),g^{\prime}(s)\in W}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{g\not=g^{\prime}\}v(g(s)-g^{\prime}(s))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C}{|W|}\int_{0}^{\beta}{\rm d}s\,\int P({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))\,\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}(s)^{2}}{|W|}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}(s)\Big),

where we denote
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}(s)=\#\{g\in\mathfrak{L}(\omega,\varpi)\colon g(s)\in W\}
, and we used the assumption (
2.23
) of superstability in Assumption (V). Jensen’s inequality gives

\langle P,F_{U}\rangle\geq\frac{C\beta}{|W|}\int{\rm d}P\,\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{G}_{W}^{2}}{|W|}-\mathfrak{G}_{W}\Big)=C\beta\int{\rm d}P\,\Big(\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{G}_{W}}{|W|}\Big)^{2}-\frac{\mathfrak{G}_{W}}{|W|}\Big),

where
\mathfrak{G}_{W}=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{\beta}{\rm d}s\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}(s)
denotes
\frac{1}{\beta}
times the total local time in
W
of all the legs in the loop/interlacements configuration. We estimate
x^{2}-x\geq\frac{1}{2}x^{2}
for
x\geq 2
and
x^{2}-x\geq-\frac{1}{4}
for
x\leq 2
; hence

\langle P,F_{U}\rangle\geq-\frac{C\beta}{4}+\frac{C\beta}{2}\int{\rm d}P\,\Big(\frac{\mathfrak{G}_{W}}{|W|}\Big)^{2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\mathfrak{G}_{W}\geq 2|W|\}.

Denote by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}
the number of particles in the entire configuration that lie in
\widetilde{W}
(regardless where its loop starts, if it is contained in a loop) and have spread
\leq M
. If
R>M
, then
\mathfrak{G}_{W}\geq\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R-M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}
, since all legs that start in
W_{R-M}
and have spread
\leq M
are entirely contained in
W=W_{R}
. Using this, we estimate

\langle P,F_{U}\rangle\geq-\frac{C\beta}{4}+\frac{C\beta}{2}\int{\rm d}P\,\Big(\frac{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R-M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}}{|W|}\Big)^{2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R-M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}\geq 2|W|\}.

Making
R\to\infty
(recalling that
W=W_{R}
) and using the spatial ergodic theorem, and making afterwards
M\to\infty
, we see that

\langle P,F_{U}\rangle\geq-\frac{C\beta}{4}+\frac{C\beta}{2}\int{\rm d}P\,\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\big)^{2}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\geq 2\}\geq\frac{C\beta}{2}\big\langle P,\big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\big)^{2}\big\rangle-\frac{5C\beta}{4}.

More precisely, by ergodicity,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R-M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}/|W|
converges
P
-almost surely to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}
as
R\to\infty
, and we can use Lebesgue’s theorem after lower estimating the integrand against
(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R-M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}/|W|)^{2}\wedge K{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W_{R-M}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,M}})}}}\geq 2|W|\}
for some
K
. Afterwards, we make
K\to\infty
and
M\to\infty
, using the monotone convergence theorem.

Now about the continuities. Recall that we are working with the topology on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
that is defined by the weak topologies induced by all the projections
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
and
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
with
W\Subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since the counting functionals are nonnegative, the lower semi-continuity is clear (for example via Fatou’s lemma). As it concerns the shred-part, the upper semi-continuity follows from the estimate, for any
K\in(0,\infty)
,

\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\leq\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\,{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\leq K\}+\frac{1}{K}\Big(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\Big)^{2},

since the
P
-integral of the last term vanishes uniformly in
P
in that set as
K\to\infty
, and the first one is a bounded and continuous function of
\Pi_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
. For the loop-part, we need to distinguish particles in loops that are entirely contained in
U
and in the other loops. The first ones are functionals of
\Pi_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
, the latter ones are functionals of
\Pi_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
. More precisely, writing
f_{x}\subset W
if
f_{x,k}(0)\in W
for all
k\in[\ell(f_{x})]
, and using the notation
N_{U}(f)=\sum_{k\in[\ell(f)]}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}_{U}(f_{k}(0))
for
f\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum_{x\in\zeta\cap U\colon f_{x}\subset U}N_{U}(f_{x})\Big){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\sum_{x\in\zeta\cap U\colon f_{x}\subset U}N_{U}(f_{x})\leq K\Big\}+\frac{1}{K}\Big(\sum_{x\in\zeta\cap U\colon f_{x}\subset U}N_{U}(f_{x})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\Big(\sum_{x\in\zeta\colon f_{x}\not\subset U}N_{U}(f_{x})\Big){\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\Big\{\sum_{x\in\zeta\colon f_{x}\not\subset U}N_{U}(f_{x})\leq K\Big\}+\frac{1}{K}\Big(\sum_{x\in\zeta\colon f_{x}\not\subset U}N_{U}(f_{x})\Big)^{2}.

The first term on the right is a bounded and continuous function of
\Pi_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\omega)
, the third is a bounded and continuous function of
\Pi_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\omega)
, and each of the two other terms are not bigger than
\frac{1}{K}\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega,\varpi)^{2}
, whose integral with respect to
P
from that set is
\leq\frac{L}{K}
. This shows the upper semi-continuity of
P\mapsto\langle P,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle
on the set of
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
satisfying
\langle P,(\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}})^{2}\rangle\leq L
.
∎

Now we proceed with the proof of Lemma
2.12
.

Proof of (1):

The compactness of
K
follows from Theorem
2.3
, noting that, for any
P\in K
and any
R\in\mathbb{N}
,

\langle\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P),(\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}})^{2}\rangle=\langle\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P),(\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}})^{2}\rangle\leq|W_{R}|\sum_{y\in Z_{R,1/2}}\langle\Pi_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P),(\mathfrak{N}_{y+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}})^{2}\rangle\leq|W_{R}|^{2}L,

using that the distribution of
\mathfrak{N}_{\partial W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
(under
\partial\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
) and of
\mathfrak{N}_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}
(under
\Pi_{W_{R}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
) are identical. Hence,
K
is contained in a set of the form (
2.17
) and is therefore compact, according to Theorem
2.3
.

Furthermore, the maps
P\mapsto\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle
and
P\mapsto\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle
are continuous on
K
, which can be proved similarly to Lemma
7.1
.

From Lemma
7.1
it directly follows that the value of the variational formula on the right-hand side of (
2.24
) with restriction to ergodic
P
s does not depend on the
L
in the definition of the set
K
, if the variational problem in (
2.24
) is restricted to
K
and
L
is large enough. According to Lemma
7.6
, for every
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
with finite entropy and energy and particle density there is an ergodic measure
\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
with hardly differing values of all these quantities, therefore the statement is also true for (
2.24
) without restrictions.

Proof of (2):
Proving the convexities of
\chi
and
\widetilde{\chi}
are elementary exercises, which we leave to the reader; they rely on the convexity of the maps
P\mapsto\langle P,F_{U}\rangle
and
P\mapsto{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
. Continuity of
\overline{\chi}
and of
\chi(\rho_{1},\cdot)
and
\chi(\cdot,\rho_{2})
in
(0,\infty)
for fixed
\rho_{1}
, respectively
\rho_{2}
, follows from convexity. The limiting statements as
\rho_{1}\to\infty
and
\rho_{2}\to\infty
follow directly from Lemma
7.1
after using Jensen’s inequality.

Proof of (3):
For
\rho_{1}=0=\rho_{2}
, only the empty measure (the shift-invariant distribution of loops and interlacements that has none of them) is admissible in the variational problem. When examining its value in
{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\cdot)
, we first see that the interlacement part is zero, since also the reference measure
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
is empty. Furthermore, the
{\tt Q}
-part is equal to the entropy
H(0\mid(q_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}})
(see (
2.6
)) of the zero sequence
m=(m_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}=(0)_{k}=0
with respect to
(q_{k})_{k}=(\frac{1}{k}4\pi\beta k)^{-d/2})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, and this entropy is equal to
q=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}q_{k}=(4\pi\beta)^{-d/2}\zeta(1+\frac{d}{2})
with
\zeta
the Riemann zeta function (recall (
1.12
)). That is,
\chi(0,0)=q
. For proving the second assertion, we need to upper bound
\frac{1}{\rho_{1}}(\chi(\rho_{1},0)-\chi(0,0))
and make
\rho_{1}\downarrow 0
. We get an upper bound for
\chi(\rho_{1},0)
by inserting the marked Poisson point process
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({m}})}}}
, defined as the reference process
{\tt Q}
, but with
(q_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
replaced by
m=(m_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}=\rho_{1}\delta_{1}=(\rho_{1},0,0,\dots)
, which is admissible in the variational formula. Since the interaction potential
v
is bounded, the expected interaction of
{\tt Q}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({m}})}}}
is not larger than
C\rho_{1}^{2}
for some
C\in(0,\infty)
and all sufficiently small
\rho_{1}
. Its relative entropy density with respect to
{\tt Q}
is equal to
H(m\mid(q_{k})_{k})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}q_{k}-\rho_{1}+\rho_{1}\log\frac{\rho_{1}}{q_{1}}
. Hence,

\frac{1}{\rho_{1}}(\chi(\rho_{1},0)-\chi(0,0))\leq\frac{1}{\rho_{1}}\Big(C\rho_{1}^{2}-\rho_{1}+\rho_{1}\log\frac{\rho_{1}}{q_{1}}\Big)\leq\log\frac{\rho_{1}}{q_{1}},

which tends to
-\infty
as
\rho_{1}\downarrow 0
. By noting that
\chi(\rho_{1},0)\geq\chi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({v=0}})}}}(\rho_{1},0)
, which tends to
\chi(0,0)
as
\rho_{1}\downarrow 0
, we also see that
\chi(\rho_{1},0)
is continuous in
\rho_{1}=0
.

Proof of (4) and (5):
The two assertions (4) and (5) rely on the same standard arguments, which are based on the assertions in (1) and the lower semi-continuity of the two maps
P\mapsto\langle P,F_{U}\rangle
and
P\mapsto{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
, the latter according to Theorem
2.3
. We leave the details to the reader.

7.2.
Preparation: entropy decomposition

Let us have a general look at the entropy terms that appear in the definition (
6.13
) of
J_{W}(\xi)
with respect to the reference measures, and decompose them into its pieces, first for loops. The next two lemmas will be jointly used in later sections for loop-interlacement distributions
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
according to the formula

\displaystyle|W|J_{W}(\xi)

\displaystyle=H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi\,\big|\,\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]\big)

(7.2)

\displaystyle=H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}\big(\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi)\mid\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\big)+\int_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi)({\rm d}\omega)\,H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{\omega}\,\big|\,\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W}\big),

writing
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{\omega}
for the kernel
\xi_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(\omega,\cdot)
.

The entropy of a random loop configuration with respect to the Poisson loop reference measure (the Brownian loop soup) in a set
W
is decomposed into the following partial entropies according to the random mechanisms:

(1)

the expected number of
k
-length loops in
W
as a sequence in
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

(2)

the number of
k
-length loops in
W
for any
k
,

(3)

the locations of the starting sites of all these loops, and finally

(4)

the loops themselves.

We write
\omega=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{x\in\zeta_{k}}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}
for loop configurations, with
\zeta_{k}=\{x\in\zeta\colon\ell(f_{x})=k\}
. Furthermore,
\mathfrak{N}_{W}(\omega)
denotes the number of loops in
W
and
\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\delta_{k}}})}}}(\omega)
the number of loops of length
k
in
W
. Let
m_{W,k}(\xi)=\frac{1}{|W|}\int\xi({\rm d}\omega)\,\mathfrak{N}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\delta_{k}}})}}}_{W}(\omega)
denote the normalized expected number of loops of length
k
in
W
under
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W})
. The Poisson parameter for
\Pi_{W,k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
, the projection of the PPP
{\tt Q}
on the set of
k
-length loops with all particles in
W
, is equal to

q_{W,k}=\frac{1}{k}\int_{W}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,k,{\rm par}}})}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}})=\frac{1}{k}\int_{W}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}(\tau_{W}>k),

(7.3)

where we recall that
\mu_{x,y}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,k,{\rm par}}})}}}
is the (not normalized) Brownian bridge measure from
x
to
y
with all the particles in
W
, and we recall from (
5.2
) that
\beta\tau_{W}
denotes the first time
\in\beta\mathbb{N}
of an exit from
W
. We write
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
for the set of
k
-length loop configurations in
W
. Introduce the projection
\Pi_{k}\colon{\mathcal{L}}\to{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
defined by
\Pi_{k}(\omega)=\sum_{x\in\zeta}{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\ell(f_{x})=k\}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}=\sum_{x\in\zeta_{k}}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}
. By
\overline{\nu}=\nu/\nu({\mathcal{X}})
we define the normalised version of a positive and finite measure
\nu
on a Polish space
{\mathcal{X}}
.

Lemma 7.2

(Entropy of loop distributions)
.

For any box
W\Subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W})
,

\displaystyle H

\displaystyle{}_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}\big(\xi\mid\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\big)

(7.4)

\displaystyle=|W|\,H\big((m_{k})_{k}\,\big|\,\textstyle{\frac{1}{|W|}}(q_{k})_{k}\big)+H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}\Big(\xi\,\Big|\,\bigotimes_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\Pi_{k}(\xi)\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}H_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(\Pi_{k}(\xi)\circ\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{-1}\mid{\rm Poi}_{m_{k}|W|})

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\Pi_{k}(\xi)(\mathfrak{N}_{W}=n)\,H\big(\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}}(\xi)\circ(\zeta_{k}\cap W)^{-1}\mid\overline{{\rm Leb}}_{W}^{\otimes n}\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}}(\xi)(\zeta_{k}\cap W\in{\rm d}A)\,\Big[H\Big(\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)\,\Big|\,\bigotimes_{x\in A}\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)\Big)+\sum_{x\in A}H\big(\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)\mid\overline{\mu}_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,k,{\rm par}}})}}}\big)\Big],

where we abbreviate
m_{k}=m_{W,k}(\xi)
and
q_{k}=m_{W,k}(\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}))
and denote by
\Pi_{k},\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}},\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}},\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
the projections on the set of loops of length
k
, respectively on the set of configurations in
W
with precisely
n
loops of length
k
and no loops else, respectively on the set of configurations of loops of length
k
with set of initial sites equal to
A\subset W
.

Proof.

We write
\Pi_{W,k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\circ\Pi_{k}=\Pi_{k}\circ\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
. In the following we drop the super-index
{\mathcal{L}}
. Note that
\Pi_{W}({\tt Q})
is equal to the convolution of the measures
\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q})
over
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Then one sees elementarily that

H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}(\xi\mid\Pi_{W}({\tt Q}))=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}}(\Pi_{k}(\xi)\mid\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q}))+H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}\Big(\xi\,\Big|\,\bigotimes_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\Pi_{k}(\xi)\Big).

(7.5)

Let us further decompose
H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}}(\Pi_{k}(\xi)\mid\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q}))
. We write a configuration in
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k}})}}}
as
\omega_{k}=\sum_{x\in\zeta_{k}\cap W}\delta_{(x,f_{x})}
. Note that
|\zeta_{k}\cap W|
has the distribution
{\rm Poi}_{q_{W,k}}
under
\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q})
, where we recall (
1.4
). By the properties of a Poisson point process,
\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q})
can be decomposed as

\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q})({\rm d}\omega_{k})={\rm Poi}_{q_{W,k}}(n)\,\overline{{\rm Leb}}_{W}^{\otimes n}({\rm d}A)\,\bigotimes_{x\in A}\overline{\mu}_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,W}})}}}({\rm d}f_{x}),\qquad n=\mathfrak{N}_{W}(\omega_{k})=|A|,A=\zeta_{k}\cap W.

(7.6)

We now write
\Pi_{k}(\xi)
in an analogous manner. We abbreviate
\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}}(\xi)
for the conditional distribution under
\Pi_{k}(\xi)
given that
|\zeta_{k}\cap W|=n
and
\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)
for the conditional distribution under
\Pi_{k}(\xi)
given that
\zeta_{k}\cap W=A
, and
\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)
for the distribution of any
f_{x}
under
\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)
. Write
m_{k}=\frac{1}{|W|}\Pi_{k}(\xi)(\mathfrak{N}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{W})
for the normalised expected particle number in the configuration under
\Pi_{k}(\xi)
in
W
. Then we can write, with the same notation as above,

\displaystyle\Pi_{k}(\xi)({\rm d}\omega_{k})

\displaystyle={{\rm Poi}}_{m_{k}|W|}(n)\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k}(\xi)\circ\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{-1}}{{\rm d}{{\rm Poi}}_{m_{k}|W|}}(n)\,\overline{{\rm Leb}}_{W}^{\otimes n}({\rm d}A)\,\frac{{\rm d}\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}}_{k}(\xi)\circ(\zeta_{k}\cap W)^{-1}}{{\rm d}\overline{{\rm Leb}}_{W}^{\otimes n}}(A)

\displaystyle\times\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)}{{\rm d}\bigotimes_{x\in A}\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)}\big((f_{x})_{x\in A}\big)\bigotimes_{x\in A}\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)}{{\rm d}\overline{\mu}_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,k,{\rm par}}})}}}}(f_{x})\,\bigotimes_{x\in A}{\overline{\mu}_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W,k,{\rm par}}})}}}}({\rm d}f_{x}).

Hence, we see that the density is given by

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k}(\xi)}{{\rm d}\Pi_{W,k}({\tt Q})}(\omega_{k})=

\displaystyle\frac{{\rm d}{{\rm Poi}}_{m_{k}|W|}(n)}{{\rm d}{{\rm Poi}}_{q_{W,k}}(n)}\,\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k}(\xi)\circ\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{-1}}{{\rm d}{{\rm Poi}}_{m_{k}|W|}}(n)\,\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n}})}}}(\xi)\circ(\zeta_{k}\cap W)^{-1}}{{\rm d}\overline{{\rm Leb}}_{W}^{\otimes n}}(A)

\displaystyle\times\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)}{{\rm d}\bigotimes_{x\in A}\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)}\big((f_{x})_{x\in A}\big)\,\prod_{x\in A}\frac{{\rm d}\Pi_{k,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({A}})}}}(\xi)}{{\rm d}\overline{\mu}_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,W}})}}}}(f_{x}).

Now take the logarithm and integrate with respect to
\Pi_{k}(\xi)({\rm d}\omega_{k})
and use (
7.5
), to obtain the assertion in (
7.4
).
∎

Now we do the same for the shreds. We write
\xi_{W}
for the kernel
\xi_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}
. We also recall the notation

q_{x,y}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n,W}})}}}({\rm d}f)=\mathbb{P}_{x}(B\in{\rm d}f\mid\tau_{W}=n,B_{\tau\beta}=y),\qquad n\in\mathbb{N},x\in W,y\in W^{\rm c},

(7.7)

for the distribution of an
n
-leg
W
-shred from
x
to
y
(which appears in the definition (
2.5
) of
{\tt K}_{W}
).

Lemma 7.3

(Entropy of shred distributions)
.

For any box
W\Subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{S}}_{W})
,

\displaystyle H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi\,\big|\,\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W}\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)({\rm d}\mu)\,H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\Big(\xi(\mu,\cdot)\,\Big|\,\bigotimes_{i\in I}\Pi_{i}(\xi(\mu,\cdot))\Big)

(7.8)

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)({\rm d}\mu)\,\sum_{i\in I}H_{{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{i}}}\big(\Pi_{i}(\xi(\mu,\cdot))\,\big|\,q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W}})}}}\big),

where we wrote
\mu=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
and
\Pi_{i}\colon{\mathcal{C}}^{I}\to{\mathcal{C}}
for the canonical projection on the
i
-th factor and
\xi(\mu,\cdot)
for the kernel
(\xi)_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(\mu,\cdot)
.

Proof.

We drop all super-indices
{\mathcal{S}}
. Then we see that

\displaystyle H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}

\displaystyle\big(\xi\,\big|\,\partial\Pi_{W}(\xi)\otimes{\tt K}_{W}\big)=\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\partial\Pi_{W}(\xi)({\rm d}\mu)\,H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi(\mu,\cdot)\,\big|\,{\tt K}_{W}(\mu,\cdot)\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\partial\Pi_{W}(\xi)({\rm d}\mu)\,\Big[H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\Big(\xi(\mu,\cdot)\,\Big|\,\bigotimes_{i\in I}\Pi_{i}(\xi(\mu,\cdot))\Big)+\sum_{i\in I}H_{{\mathcal{C}}_{l_{i}}}\big(\Pi_{i}(\xi(\mu,\cdot))\,\big|\,q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W}})}}}\big)\Big].

∎

7.3.
Proof of Lemma
2.13

In this section, we prove Lemma
2.13
, which says that
\chi(\rho_{1}+\rho_{2},0)\leq\chi(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
for any
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in[0,\infty)
. For this, we will replace an arbitrary loop-interlacements configuration by a loop configuration without changing much the number of particles per volume and without increasing the energy nor the entropy terms. We do this by rewiring the ends of all the shreds at the boundary of a large box
W=W_{R}
in such a way that all the shreds of interlacements are turned into the shreds of one long loop just outside that box, i.e., in
W_{R+M}\setminus W_{R}
. The new configuration has only loops in
W_{R+M}
and no shreds. Our manipulation does not change anything inside
W_{R}
, which is the main part, and the changes in
W_{R+M}\setminus W_{R}
have only a vanishing influence in the limit as
R\to\infty
, as it concerns the three main quantities: entropy, expected interaction energy per volume and particle densities. Finally, we put independent copies of the new configuration into the boxes
z+W_{R+M}
,
z\in 2(R+M)\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and mix the configuration in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
over
x\in W_{R+M}
and control the entropy of this configuration.

We need some more notation. We need some large parameters
R,M,T\in(1,\infty)
. As usual, we write
W=[-R,R]^{d}
. Denote by
\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W}
the natural projection on loops, respectively shreds, whose legs
f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{1}
have throughout spread
\|f\|_{\rm sp}=\max_{t\in[0,\beta]}|f(t)-f(0)|<M
. We denote by
\widetilde{J}_{W;M}
the normalized entropy function as in (
6.13
), but with the reference measure
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]
replaced by its image measure under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}
. Note that
\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\xi\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1})\leq J_{W}(\xi)
for any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
, by monotonicity of the relative entropy in the reference
\sigma
-field, see
[
G11
, Proposition 15.5(c)]
.

Furthermore, we write
\widetilde{W}=W_{R+M}=[-R-M,R+M]^{d}
and denote by
A_{\widetilde{W};M,T}=\{(\omega,\varpi)\in{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{\widetilde{W}}\times{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{\widetilde{W}}\colon\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+U}(\omega,\varpi)\leq T\,\forall z\in{\widetilde{W}}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}
, where we recall that
U=[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
is the unit box and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{z+U}
denotes the number of particles in the loop/shred configuration in
z+U
, regardless where the loop starts; see also Remark
2.11
. (This event is very similar to
A_{{\widetilde{W}};\frac{1}{2},M,K,L,T}
defined in (
4.36
).) Furthermore, let
\widetilde{\Pi}_{{\widetilde{W}};M,T}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{W}}\to A_{{\widetilde{W}};M,T}
be some projection onto
A_{{\widetilde{W}};M,T}
.

Let us explain the structure of our proof.
We may assume that
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}\in(0,\infty)
. Pick
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}})
with
\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle=\rho_{1}
and
\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle=\rho_{2}
. Furthermore, fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Pick
M
and
T
so large that
\langle\Pi_{U}(P),\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T}\rangle\geq\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}-\varepsilon
, where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}+\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
is the number of all particles in loops and in shreds in
U
.

We are going to prove that, for any sufficiently large
M
and
T
, and for any sufficiently large
R\in(1,\infty)
, there exists some ergodic
\widetilde{P}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}})
such that
|\langle\Pi_{U}(\widetilde{P}),\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\ell}})}}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T}\rangle-\langle\Pi_{U}(P),\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T}\rangle|\leq\varepsilon
and
\langle\frac{1}{|\widetilde{W}|}F_{\widetilde{W},\widetilde{W}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{\widetilde{W};M,T},\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P})\rangle\leq\langle\frac{1}{|W|}F_{W,W}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M,T},\Pi_{W}(P)\rangle+\varepsilon
and
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}))\leq\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\Pi_{W}(P)\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1})+\varepsilon
.

Indeed, this implies for any
\varepsilon
and all sufficiently large
M
and
T
and for any sufficiently large
R
that

\displaystyle\inf\Big\{

\displaystyle\Big\langle\frac{1}{|W_{R+M}|}F_{W_{R+M},W_{R+M}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W_{R+M};M,T},\Pi_{W_{R+M}}(P)\Big\rangle+J_{W_{R+M}}(\Pi_{W_{R+M}}(P))\colon

\displaystyle P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}})\mbox{ ergodic},\langle\Pi_{U}(P),\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\ell}})}}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T}\rangle\in{\mathcal{B}}_{\varepsilon}(\rho_{1}+\rho_{2})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon+\inf\Big\{\Big\langle\frac{1}{|W|}F_{W,W}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M,T},\Pi_{W}(P)\Big\rangle+\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\Pi_{W}(P)\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1})\colon

\displaystyle P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}}\times{\mathcal{S}}),\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\rangle=\rho_{1},\langle P,\mathfrak{N}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\rangle=\rho_{2}\Big\}.

The right-hand side is not larger than
3\varepsilon+\chi(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
, as we show now. Indeed, clearly
F_{W,W}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M,T}\leq F_{W,W}
. Furthermore, splitting all the contributions to
F_{W,W}
, one sees that, for any
\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}
and
\varpi\in{\mathcal{S}}
,

\displaystyle F_{W,W}(\Pi_{W}(\omega,\varpi))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in W\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}F_{z+U,z+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}+F_{z+U,z+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}+\frac{1}{2}F_{z+U,z+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}

(7.9)

\displaystyle+F_{z+U,z+U^{\rm c}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}+F_{z+U,z+U^{\rm c}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}+F_{z+U^{\rm c},z+U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}+F_{z+U,z+U^{\rm c}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\Big](\omega,\varpi).

Now integrate both sides with respect to
P
and use its shift-invariance, to see that
\langle\frac{1}{|W|}F_{W,W}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M,T},P\rangle\leq\langle F_{U},P\rangle
, where we recall (
2.22
) for the definition of
F_{U}
. Furthermore,
\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\Pi_{W}(P)\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1})\leq\varepsilon+J_{W}(\Pi_{W}(P))\leq\varepsilon+{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
by (
2.16
).

The left-hand side converges, as
R,M,T\to\infty
and
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
to
\chi(\rho_{1}+\rho_{2},0)
, as we show now. Indeed, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and any ergodic
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}})
with
\langle P,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\ell}})}}}\rangle=\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}
and finite values of
{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(P\mid{\tt Q})
and
\langle F_{U},P\rangle
, pick
M
and
T
so large that
P
is amenable to the formula on the left and such that
\langle F_{U}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T},P\rangle\geq\langle F_{U},P\rangle-\varepsilon
. Making
R\to\infty
and using Theorem
2.3
, we see that
J_{W_{R+M}}(\Pi_{W_{R+M}}(P))
converges towards
{\rm h}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(P\mid{\tt Q})
. Furthermore, making
R\to\infty
we see that
\langle\frac{1}{|W_{R+M}|}F_{W_{R+M},W_{R+M}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W_{R+M};M,T},\Pi_{W_{R+M}}(P)\rangle
converges towards
\langle F_{U}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T},P\rangle
.
Since
\chi(\rho_{1}+\rho_{2},0)
is the infimum over all
P\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}})
(not only the ergodic ones), we have arrived at the desired estimate,
\chi(\rho_{1}+\rho_{2},0)\leq\chi(\rho_{1},\rho_{2})
.

Here is our construction of
\widetilde{P}
. Recall

W=W_{R}=[-R,R]^{d}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\widetilde{W}=W_{R+M}=[-R-M,R+M]^{d}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad W^{\prime}=\widetilde{W}\setminus W.

Consider
\xi_{R}=\Pi_{W}(P)\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
. Furthermore, assume that
M
is also so large that

\frac{{\rm d}\nu}{{\rm d}\nu\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1}}(\omega,\varpi)\leq(1+\varepsilon)^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega,\varpi)},\qquad R>1,(\omega,\varpi)\in{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W},

(7.10)

where
\nu=\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]
(this is possible since all the leg spreads are i.i.d. under
\nu
). We are going to construct from
\xi_{R}
some loop-configuration measure
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}})
as follows. First, we add an empty loop configuration
\delta_{\underline{0}}
in
W^{\prime}=\widetilde{W}\setminus W
(i.e., no loop starts in
W^{\prime}
). Furthermore, we extend all the initial and terminal sites of all the
W
-shreds of
\xi_{R}
by appending independent Brownian bridge pieces with particles only within
W^{\prime}
such that a single large loop in
\widetilde{W}
arises. Hence,
\Pi_{W}(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})=\Pi_{W}(\xi_{R})=\xi_{R}
, and
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
has no
\widetilde{W}
-shreds. Then we decompose
\mathbb{R}^{d}
regularly into the boxes
z+\widetilde{W}
with
z\in 2(R+M)\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and put into each such subbox one independent copy of a loop-configuration with distribution
\theta_{z}(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
(where
\theta_{z}
is the shift-operator such that
\theta_{z}(z)=0
). Call the distribution of this configuration
\widetilde{P}_{R,M}=\bigotimes_{z\in 2(R+M)\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\theta_{z}(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
and define
P_{R,M}=\frac{1}{|W_{R+M}|}\int_{W_{R+M}}{\rm d}x\,\widetilde{P}_{R,M}\circ\theta_{x}^{-1}
. Then
P_{R,M}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\rm s}})}}}({\mathcal{L}})
is obviously stationary and contains no interlacement. The proof of
[
G11
, Theorem 14.12]
applies and shows that
P_{R,M}
is ergodic.

We need to show the following:

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}\langle P_{R,M},\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\ell}})}}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T}\rangle-\langle P,[\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}+\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{U}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}]\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{U;M,T}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(7.11)

\displaystyle\limsup_{R\to\infty}\Big[\Big\langle\frac{1}{|\widetilde{W}|}F_{\widetilde{W},\widetilde{W}}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{\widetilde{W};M,T},P_{R,M}\Big\rangle-\Big\langle\frac{1}{|W|}F_{W,W}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M,T},P\Big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 0,

(7.12)

\displaystyle\limsup_{R\to\infty}[J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P_{R,M}))-\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\Pi_{W}(P)\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon.

(7.13)

Then
\widetilde{P}=P_{R,M}
with sufficiently large
R
is suitable, and the assertion follows.

Properties (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) are easy to show and do not need details of the construction:

Proof of (
7.11
):
Since
|\widetilde{W}|/|W|\to 1
, it suffices to show that
\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}},\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{\widetilde{W}}\circ\Pi_{\widetilde{W};M,T}\rangle=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\xi_{R},[\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\ell}})}}}+\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\ell}})}}}]\circ\Pi_{W;M,T}\rangle
. Recall that
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
has its particles from (1) the loops of
\xi_{R}
in
W
, (2) the shreds of
\xi_{R}
in
W
, and (3) the added shreds in
W^{\prime}
. Therefore, it suffices to show that the expected amount of the particles of (3) is negligible in the limit
R\to\infty
. But this is clear since
\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W^{\prime}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\circ\Pi_{W^{\prime};M,T}\leq|W^{\prime}|T\leq CMT|W|/R
for some constant
C
that depends only on
d
.

Proof of (
7.12
):
Again, we only have to show that the expected
F_{W^{\prime},\widetilde{W}}\circ\Pi_{\widetilde{W};M,T}
-interactions of the added Brownian shreds in
W^{\prime}
with itself and with any other part of the configuration in
\widetilde{W}
are
o(|W|)
as
R\to\infty
. But this is again clear, since
F_{W^{\prime},\widetilde{W}}\circ\Pi_{\widetilde{W};M,T}\leq C_{M,T}|W|/R
for some constant
C_{M,T}
that depends only on
M
and
T
(and possibly on
\beta
,
d
and
v
); see Lemma
4.4
.

Now let us show that property (
7.13
) is satisfied.

Let us first make a connection between
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P_{R,M}))
and
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M}))
(the latter is
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
). By convexity of
J_{\widetilde{W}}
and by (
6.17
) in Proposition
6.6
(1) with
m=3
(it is clear that
\widetilde{P}_{R,M}
is
\widetilde{W}
-ergodic and hence
3\widetilde{W}
-ergodic), we have, for all
R
sufficiently large,

\displaystyle J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P_{R,M}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|\widetilde{W}|}\int_{\widetilde{W}}{\rm d}x\,J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M}\circ\theta_{x}^{-1}))=\frac{1}{|\widetilde{W}|}\int_{\widetilde{W}}{\rm d}x\,J_{\widetilde{W}}(\theta_{x}(\Pi_{x+\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M})))

(7.14)

\displaystyle\leq J_{W_{3(R+M)}}(\Pi_{W_{3(R+M)}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M}))+\varepsilon,

where we used that, by construction,
\widetilde{P}_{R,M}
has no shred-part and therefore has
\psi=0
for
\psi=\partial\Pi_{W_{3(R+M)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M})
.

Since, by construction,
\Pi_{W_{3(R+M)}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M})
and also the reference measure in the entropy
J_{W_{3(R+M)}}
, decomposes into the product of
3^{d}
shifted copies of
\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M})
(recall that it has no loops in
z+W^{\prime}
for all the
z\in 2(R+M)\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and therefore an empty boundary-shred part at the boundaries of all these subboxes
z+\widetilde{W}
), the right-hand side is not larger than
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M}))+\varepsilon
. Hence, it will be sufficient to prove (
7.13
) with
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(\widetilde{P}_{R,M}))=J_{\widetilde{W}}(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})
instead of
J_{\widetilde{W}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P_{R,M}))
. More explicitly, we need to show that
\limsup_{R\to\infty}[J_{\widetilde{W}}(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})-\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\xi_{R})]\leq 0
. Recall that the reference measure for the entropy of
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
is
\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
, while the one for
\xi_{R}
is equal to
[\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]]\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1}
.

However, proving property (
7.13
) needs the details of the construction of
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
from
\xi_{R}
, which we are going to describe now. Fix a configuration
(\omega,\varpi)\in{\mathcal{L}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}_{W}
. Write
\varpi=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{f_{i}}
and
\mu=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\varpi)=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
with
l_{i}=\ell(f_{i})
and
f_{i}(0)=x_{i}
and
f_{i}(\beta l_{i})=y_{i}
. Then
x_{i}\in W
and
y_{i}\in W^{\prime}
for any
i\in I
, since each leg of a loop in
\varpi
has spread
\leq M
(we consider
\varpi
under the distribution
\xi_{R}
, which has this property almost surely). With some bijection
\sigma\colon I\to I
that has only one cycle, we sample, conditionally given
\mu
, a collection of random lengths
(L_{i})_{i\in I}
with distribution
\bigotimes_{i\in I}p_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}
, where we define

p_{y,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(n)=\mathbb{P}_{y}(\tau_{W^{\prime}}=n\mid B_{\tau_{W^{\prime}}\beta}=x),\qquad y\in W^{\prime},x\in(W^{\prime})^{\rm c},n\in\mathbb{N},

(7.15)

(compare to (
5.5
)). Then we append, for
i\in I
, an
L_{i}
-length Brownian bridge
B_{i}
with particles only in
W^{\prime}
at
y_{i}
terminating at
x_{\sigma(i)}
. In other words, each
B_{i}
has distribution
p_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}\otimes q_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\cdot,W^{\prime}}})}}}
, where

q^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n,W^{\prime}}})}}}_{y,x}({\rm d}f)=\mathbb{P}_{y}\big((B_{s})_{s\in[0,n\beta]}\in{\rm d}f\mid\tau_{W^{\prime}}=n,B_{n\beta}=x\big),\qquad y\in W^{\prime},x\in(W^{\prime})^{\rm c},n\in\mathbb{N},f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n},

(7.16)

(compare to (
5.3
),
\tau_{W^{\prime}}
was defined in (
5.2
)), and
(B_{i})_{i\in I}
is independent.

In this way, all shreds of
\xi_{R}
are turned into a single loop
f\in{\mathcal{C}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\circlearrowleft}})}}}
in
\widetilde{W}
with length
\ell(f)=n=\sum_{i}(l_{i}+L_{i})
. By default, we take the starting site as
x_{i^{*}}\in W
, where
i^{*}
is uniformly distributed over
I
. This loop runs subsequently through the functions
f_{{i^{*}}}
,
g_{{i^{*}}}
,
f_{{\sigma(i^{*})}}
,
g_{{\sigma(i^{*})}}
,
f_{{\sigma^{2}(i^{*})}}
,
g_{{\sigma^{2}(i^{*})}}
and so on, more precisely, it is the concatenation of these functions in this order. The change from one
f_{i}
to some
g_{i}
happens at the stopping times where the next particle
\in W^{\prime}
appears, and the changes from
g_{j}
to
f_{{\sigma(j)}}
happen at the stopping times when the next particle
\in W
appears. Finally, we define
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
as the distribution of
\omega+\delta_{f}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}
under
\delta_{\underline{0}}\otimes\xi_{R}({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))\otimes\bigotimes_{i\in I}[p_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}\otimes q_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\cdot,W^{\prime}}})}}}]
.

Let us first identify the reference measure
\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
explicitly. Recall from Section
4.1
that
{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}={\mathcal{L}}_{W\cup W^{\prime}}
consists of the three parts
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{W^{\prime}}
and the set
{\mathcal{L}}_{W,W^{\prime}}
of loops in
\widetilde{W}=W\cup W^{\prime}
that have particles both in
W
and in
W^{\prime}
, but nowhere else. The reference measure
\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
is equal to the convolution of
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
,
\Pi_{W^{\prime}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
and
\Pi_{W,W^{\prime}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
. Observe that
\Pi_{W,W^{\prime}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})({\rm d}\delta_{f})
, having just one loop with starting site at
x_{i^{*}}
with length
n
, can be decomposed, according to the Markov property. Therefore, using also (
7.3
) and

q_{W,W^{\prime},k}=\frac{1}{k}\int_{W}{\rm d}x\,\mu_{x,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({k,W\cup W^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{W,W^{\prime}}\geq 1),

(7.17)

where we recall that
\mathfrak{m}_{W,W^{\prime}}
is the number of times that the Brownian loop changes between
W
and
W^{\prime}
,
and abbreviating

A_{\sigma}\big({\rm d}\mu\big)=\bigotimes_{i\in I}\Big[\mathbb{P}_{x_{i}}(\tau_{W}=l_{i},B(\tau_{W}\beta)\in{\rm d}y_{i})\mathbb{P}_{y_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}}(B(\tau_{W^{\prime}}\beta)\in{\rm d}x_{i})\Big],

(7.18)

we can write

\displaystyle\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}

\displaystyle({\tt Q})\big({\rm d}(\omega+\underline{0}+\delta_{f})\big)=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})({\rm d}\omega)\,{\operatorname{e}}^{-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}q_{W^{\prime},k}}\,{\operatorname{e}}^{-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}[q_{W,W^{\prime},k}+q_{W^{\prime},W,k}]}q_{W,W^{\prime},n}B_{n}

(7.19)

\displaystyle\otimes A_{\sigma}({\rm d}\mu)\otimes\bigotimes_{i\in I}\Big(q_{x_{i},y_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({l_{i},W}})}}}({\rm d}f_{i})\otimes q_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({L_{i},W^{\prime}}})}}}({\rm d}g_{i})\Big),\qquad\mbox{with }B_{n}=\frac{1}{|I|}\sum_{i\in I}\frac{1}{\mu_{x_{i},x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n,W\cup W^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{W,W^{\prime}}\geq 1)}.

Indeed, the exponential terms in the first line are the probability that no
W^{\prime}
-loop exists and that one
n
-loop in
W\cup W^{\prime}
with particles both in
W
and in
W^{\prime}
exists and no loop else; the product of the measures over
i
is the probability distribution of the
W
-shreds and the
W^{\prime}
-shreds of the loop
f
(because of the cyclic structure and the uniform choice of the starting site, one does not see here the starting site explicitly); and
B(n)
is the normalization for a length-
n
loop in
W\cup W^{\prime}
that has particles both in
W
and in
W^{\prime}
and starts from some point that is uniformly distributed over
\{x_{i}\colon i\in I\}
.

Accordingly, also
\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}
splits into these three parts, observing also the dependences on
\omega
and
\delta_{f}
and recalling that we added no loop that starts in
W^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}

\displaystyle\big({\rm d}(\omega+\underline{0}+\delta_{f})\big)=\xi_{R}\big({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi)\big)\,\,{\operatorname{e}}^{-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}[q_{W^{\prime},k}+q_{W^{\prime},W,k}]}\bigotimes_{i\in I}\Big[p_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(L_{i})\,q_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({L_{i},W^{\prime}}})}}}({\rm d}g_{i})\Big]

(7.20)

\displaystyle=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega)\,{\operatorname{e}}^{-\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}[q_{W^{\prime},k}+q_{W^{\prime},W,k}]}\,(\xi_{R})_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(\omega,{\rm d}\varpi)\otimes\bigotimes_{i\in I}\Big[p_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(L_{i})\,q_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({L_{i},W^{\prime}}})}}}({\rm d}g_{i})\Big],

where we recall that
\varpi=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{f_{i}}
and
\mu=\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\varpi)=\sum_{i\in I}\delta_{(x_{i},l_{i},y_{i})}
.
Then, writing
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}
for the projection on
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{W}
,

\displaystyle H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}}

\displaystyle(\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}\mid\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}))=H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}}(\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})\mid\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q}))

(7.21)

\displaystyle+\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}(\omega,\mu))H_{{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big((\xi_{R})_{({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{W})\to{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}(\omega,\mu,\cdot)\,\big|\,{\tt K}_{W}(\mu,\cdot)\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}q_{W,W^{\prime},k}-\int\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}({\rm d}\varpi)\,\log\big(q_{W,W^{\prime},n}B(n)\big)+\int\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega)\,H_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\big((\xi_{R})_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}(\omega,\cdot)\,\big|\,A_{\sigma}\big)

\displaystyle=H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi_{R}\,\big|\,\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]\big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}q_{W,W^{\prime},k}-\int\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}})({\rm d}\varpi)\,\log\big(q_{W,W^{\prime},n}B(n)\big)+\int\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega)\,H_{{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}\big((\xi_{R})_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}(\omega,\cdot)\,\big|\,A_{\sigma}\big),

as one sees from (
7.2
), together with a comparison with Lemma
7.3
. Now divide this by
|W_{R+M}|
and make
R\to\infty
and observe that
|W_{R+M}|/|W_{R}|\to 1
. For the entropy of
\xi_{R}
, we need to switch from the reference measure
\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]
to its image measure under
\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}
. This gives for the first term on the right-hand side of (
7.21
), using (
7.10
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|W|}

\displaystyle H_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}}\big(\xi_{R}\,\big|\,\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]\big)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\xi_{R})+\frac{1}{|W|}\int\xi_{R}({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))\,\log\frac{{\rm d}\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]}{{\rm d}\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{W}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}_{W}(\xi_{R})\otimes{\tt K}_{W}]\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}^{-1}}(\omega,\varpi)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\xi_{R})+\frac{1}{|W|}\int\Pi_{W}(P)({\rm d}(\omega,\varpi))\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{N}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}(\omega,\varpi)\log(1+\varepsilon)\leq\widetilde{J}_{W;M}(\xi_{R})+C\varepsilon,

where
C
depends only on
\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}
.

We have to show that all other terms on the right-hand side of (
7.21
) vanish (i.e., have a vanishing upper bound) with a prefactor of
1/|W|
as
R\to\infty
. We leave the proof of
\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{|W_{R}|}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}q_{W,W^{\prime},k}=0
to the reader.

Let us turn to the next term. Recall that
n=\sum_{i\in I}(l_{i}+L_{i})
is random. A lower bound for
B_{n}
is easy to get by estimating the
\mu
-term against the free Gaussian kernel with parameter
\beta n
, i.e.,
\mu_{x_{i},x_{i}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({n,W\cup W^{\prime},{\rm par}}})}}}(\mathfrak{m}_{W,W^{\prime}}\geq 1)\leq(4\pi\beta n)^{-d/2}
, such that it is clear that
\liminf_{R\to\infty}\frac{1}{|W_{R}|}\int\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}({\rm d}\varpi)\,\log B_{n}\geq 0
. Concerning the other part of that term, we claim that we may estimate
q_{W,W^{\prime},n}\geq{\operatorname{e}}^{-nC/R^{2}}
for any large
R
and
n
. Indeed, lower bound the event
\{\mathfrak{m}_{W,W^{\prime}}\geq 1\}\cap\{B_{0}=x_{i}=B_{n\beta}\}\cap\{B_{k\beta}\in W\cup W^{\prime}\,\forall k\in[n]\}
by the event that the Brownian motion on the time interval
[0,\beta n]
, starting from and terminating at
x_{i}
, runs entirely within
W\cup W^{\prime}
and visits both
W
and
W^{\prime}
. Then, for large
n
, the probability of this event has a logarithmic asymptotics in
n
with a parameter equal to the principal Dirichlet eigenvalue of the Laplace operator in
W\cup W^{\prime}
. This eigenvalue is, as
R\to\infty
, asymptotic to some constant times
1/(R+M)^{2}\sim 1/R^{2}
. This implies that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{|W|}

\displaystyle\int\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}({\rm d}\varpi)\,\log q_{W,W^{\prime},n}\leq\frac{C}{R^{2}}\,\frac{1}{|W|}\int\xi_{R}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M}})}}}({\rm d}\varpi_{R})\,\sum_{i\in I}(l_{i}+L_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{2}}\,\frac{1}{|W|}\Big(\langle\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{R}),\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}\rangle+\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\mu)\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}mp_{y_{i},x_{\sigma(i)}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(m)\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{R^{2}}+\frac{C}{R^{2}}\frac{1}{|W|}\int\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\mu)\sum_{i\in I}|x_{\sigma(i)}-y_{i}|,

using the following Lemma
7.4
. We now estimate simply
|x_{\sigma(i)}-y_{i}|\leq CR
and
|I|\leq\sum_{i\in I}l_{i}=\mathfrak{N}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell,{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\varpi)
, to see that the last summand is of order
O(1/R)
and that the right-hand side vanishes as
R\to\infty
.

Lemma 7.4

(Expected length of Brownian bridge in
W^{\prime}
)
.

For any
M\in(0,\infty)
, there is a constant
C_{M}\in(0,\infty)
(depending on
d
,
\beta
and
M
) such that, for any
R\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}np_{y,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(n)\leq C_{M}|x-y|,\qquad y\in W^{\prime},x\in(W^{\prime})^{\rm c}.

(7.22)

Proof.

For the proof, it suffices to replace
W^{\prime}
by
[-R-M,R+M]^{d-1}\times[0,M]
(which we will still write as
W^{\prime}
in this proof), since
W_{R+M}\setminus W_{R}
is a finite union of such sets, modulo shifts and orthogonal transformations. We write
x=(\underline{x},x^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}})
with
\underline{x}\in[-R-M,R+M]^{d-1}
and
x^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}}\in[0,M]
. The assertion needs to be proved only for large
R
and large
|\underline{x}-\underline{y}|
. The intuitive reason for (
7.22
) is that
\tau_{W^{\prime}}
has exponential tails as
R\to\infty
under
\mathbb{P}_{y}(\cdot\mid B(\tau_{W^{\prime}}\beta)=x)
, uniformly in
x\in W_{R}
and
y\in W^{\prime}
, since the
d
-th component of the Brownian bridge lies in
[0,M]
. This implies that the conditioned Gaussian random walk
(B_{n\beta})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is forced to assume a drift into the direction
w(\underline{x}-\underline{y})
for some
w\in(0,\infty)
that asymptotically depends only on
M
.

Let us come to the details. The vector
(\underline{B}_{n\beta})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of the first
d-1
components and the
d
-th component
(B_{n\beta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of the Gaussian random walk are independent, and the stopping time
\tau_{W^{\prime}}
depends only on the latter; indeed, we write it as the exit time
\tau_{[0,M]}
from
[0,M]
for the
d
-th component. Let us denote the one-step distribution density of the Gaussian walk by
g
and its
(d-1)
-dimensional version by
\underline{g}
and with
d
-th component version
g^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}}
(these are nothing but the usual Gaussian densities with variance parameter
2\beta
in the respective dimensions). We now integrate over
B_{n\beta}
to get, in the limit as
R\to\infty
, uniformly in large
n
and in
y\in W^{\prime}
and
x\in(W^{\prime})^{\rm c}
for
|\underline{x}-\underline{y}|\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}_{y}(B_{\tau_{W^{\prime}}\beta}\in{\rm d}x)}{{\rm d}x}p_{y,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(n+1)

\displaystyle=\int_{W^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{y}(\tau_{W^{\prime}}>n,B_{n\beta}\in{\rm d}z)g(z-x)

\displaystyle\sim\mathbb{E}_{\underline{y}}\big[\underline{g}(\underline{B}_{n\beta}-\underline{x})\big]\,\mathbb{E}_{y^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}}}\big[{\mathchoice{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.0mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-4.5mu\mathrm{l}}{1\mskip-5.0mu\mathrm{l}}}\{\tau_{[0,M]}>n\}g^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}}(B_{n\beta}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}}-x^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}})\big]

\displaystyle={\operatorname{e}}^{-|\underline{x}-\underline{y}|^{2}/4\beta(n+1)}\,{\operatorname{e}}^{-nC_{M}}{\operatorname{e}}^{o(n)},

where the first term on the right-hand side comes out of a quadratic extension in the exponent, and the second from subadditivity (the probability of
\{\tau_{[0,M]}>n\}
decays exponentially in
n
, while the
g^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({d}})}}}
-expectation is bounded in
n
). Hence, the term
p_{y,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(n+1)
is strictly maximal for
n\sim|\underline{x}-\underline{y}|/\sqrt{4\beta C_{M}}
. Hence, the sum of
(n+1)p_{y,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(n+1)
over
n
comes mainly from the summands around that value of
n
, and hence it is asymptotic to
|\underline{x}-\underline{y}|/\sqrt{4\beta C_{M}}
. This implies the assertion in (
7.22
).

The right-hand side of the last display is equal to
{\operatorname{e}}^{-|\underline{y}-\underline{x}|(\sqrt{C_{M}/\beta}+o(1))}
for this
n
. Since the
p_{y,x}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({W^{\prime}}})}}}(n+1)
do not decay exponentially fast for
n/|\underline{x}-\underline{y}|
in a neighbourhood of
\sqrt{4\beta C_{M}}
, but away from it and sum up to one on
n
, we also see that
\mathbb{P}_{y}(B_{\tau_{W^{\prime}}\beta}\in{\rm d}x)/{\rm d}x={\operatorname{e}}^{-|\underline{y}-\underline{x}|(\sqrt{C_{M}/\beta}+o(1))}
as
|\underline{y}-\underline{x}|\to\infty
.

∎

Now let us turn to the last term on the right-hand side of (
7.21
), where we recall the definition of
A_{\sigma}
in (
7.18
). We are going to write the measure
(\xi_{R})_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}(\omega,\cdot)=\frac{\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega,\cdot)}{\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega)}
with the help of projections of the measure
\widetilde{\xi}=\widetilde{\Pi}_{\widetilde{W};M}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P))
. This measure has a finite entropy with respect to the reference measure in the definition of
J_{\widetilde{W}}
, hence, it has a density
\widetilde{g}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{W}}\to[0,\infty)
with respect to the reference measure
\widetilde{\nu}=\Pi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{\widetilde{W}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)\otimes{\tt K}_{\widetilde{W}}]
. We conceive the projections
\pi_{1}=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{W}}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{W}
and
\pi_{2}=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{W}}\to{\mathcal{L}}_{W}
as projections from configurations in
\widetilde{W}
to loop/boundary-shred-configurations in
W
, respectively to loop configurations in
W
. The factorisation lemma from measure theory implies that there are measurable functions
g_{1}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{W}\to[0,\infty)
and
g_{2}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to[0,\infty)
such that
\widetilde{g}=g_{2}\circ\pi_{2}=g_{1}\circ\pi_{1}
and
g_{1}=g_{2}\circ\pi_{3}
, where
\pi_{3}
is the projection from
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{T}}_{W}
on
{\mathcal{L}}_{W}
, that is,
g_{1}(\omega,\mu)=g_{2}(\omega)
. Therefore, they are densities of the image measures of
\widetilde{\xi}
with respect to the image measures of
\widetilde{\nu}
under
\pi_{1}
, respectively under
\pi_{2}
, that is,

g_{1}(\omega,\mu)=\frac{{\rm d}\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\xi})}{{\rm d}\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\nu})}(\omega,\mu)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad g_{2}(\omega)=\frac{{\rm d}\pi_{2}(\xi_{R})}{{\rm d}\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\nu})}(\omega),\qquad\omega\in{\mathcal{L}}_{W},\mu\in{\mathcal{T}}_{W}.

Recall from (
8.5
) that
(\xi_{R})_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}(\omega,{\rm d}\mu)=\frac{\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega,{\rm d}\mu)}{\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})({\rm d}\omega)}
and note that

\displaystyle\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})

\displaystyle=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}(\Pi_{W}(P)))=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\to W}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P))))

\displaystyle=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},{\mathcal{T}}}})}}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\to W}(\widetilde{\Pi}_{\widetilde{W};M}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}(P)))=\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\xi}),

since
\widetilde{\Pi}_{W;M}\circ\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\to W}=\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\to W}\circ\widetilde{\Pi}_{\widetilde{W};M}
(by
\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\to W}
we mean
\Pi_{W}
with domain
{\mathcal{L}}_{\widetilde{W}}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{\widetilde{W}}
). Analogously, we see that
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})=\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\xi})
. Hence,

\frac{(\xi_{R})_{{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\to{\mathcal{T}}_{W}}(\omega,{\rm d}\mu)}{A_{\sigma}({\rm d}\mu)}=\frac{\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\xi})({\rm d}\omega,{\rm d}\mu)}{\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\xi})({\rm d}\omega)A_{\sigma}({\rm d}\mu)}=\frac{g_{1}(\omega,\mu)}{g_{2}(\omega)}\,\frac{{\rm d}\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\nu})}{{\rm d}[\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\nu})\otimes A_{\sigma}]}(\omega,\mu)=\frac{{\rm d}\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\nu})}{{\rm d}[\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\nu})\otimes A_{\sigma}]}(\omega,\mu),

where the last step derives from
g_{1}=g_{2}\circ\pi_{3}
.

Note that
\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\nu})=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q}))=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
, since the remainder part of
\widetilde{\nu}
does not create loops in
W
. On the other hand,
\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\nu})=\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\setminus W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\widetilde{W}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)\otimes{\tt K}_{\widetilde{W}}])
, where we denote by
\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\setminus W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\widetilde{W}}})}}}
the projection on all the loops that start in
\widetilde{W}\setminus W
and have all the particles in
\widetilde{W}
. Therefore,

\frac{{\rm d}\pi_{1}(\widetilde{\nu})}{{\rm d}[\pi_{2}(\widetilde{\nu})\otimes A_{\sigma}]}(\omega,\mu)=\frac{{\rm d}\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\Pi_{\widetilde{W}\setminus W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}},\widetilde{W}}})}}}({\tt Q})\otimes[\partial\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)\otimes{\tt K}_{\widetilde{W}}])}{{\rm d}A_{\sigma}}(\mu).

The measure in the numerator is equal to
A_{\sigma^{\prime}}
for some random bijective
\sigma^{\prime}\colon I\to I
whose distribution depends on
\partial\Pi_{\widetilde{W}}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
. Hence, the quotient is of the form
\prod_{i\in I}\mathbb{P}_{(\sigma^{\prime})^{-1}(i)}(B(\tau_{W^{\prime}\beta})\in{\rm d}x_{i})/\mathbb{P}_{\sigma^{-1}(i)}(B(\tau_{W^{\prime}\beta})\in{\rm d}x_{i})
, which is bounded from above by
C_{M}^{|\mu|}
for some constant
C_{M}
, depending only on
M
,
d
and
\beta
. We have to integrate its logarithm with respect to
\xi_{R}
, which gives
\log C_{M}
times the expectation of
|\mu|
under
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi_{R})=\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(P)
, i.e., the expected number of
W
-shreds under
P
. This is
o(|W|)
, according to Corollary
5.8
. This shows that the last term on the right-hand side of (
7.21
) is
o(|W|)
and hence negligible.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
2.13
.

7.4.
Preparation: entropy of projection image measures

Fix
W
and recall the events
A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}
defined in (
4.34
)–(
4.36
) and the restricted shred-configuration set
{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
defined in (
5.21
) and the next line. In this section, we show that the entropy
J_{W}(\xi)
is well approximated with the entropy
J_{W}(\widetilde{\xi})
of a measure
\widetilde{\xi}
that approximates
\xi
and is concentrated on the above restricted set of configurations, if the parameters are picked large. More precisely, we estimate the entropy
J_{W}(\xi\circ\Pi_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T,\vartheta,S}^{-1})
of some projection
\Pi_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T,\vartheta,S}
of an arbitrary measure
\xi
on
A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T}\cap{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}}
in terms of
J_{W}(\xi)
. We want to show that the projected entropy is not much larger than the original entropy after taking
W\uparrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
M,L,K
and
T,\vartheta\to\infty
, uniformly in
S\in\mathbb{N}
. This will be used in the proof of the ergodic approximation in Lemma
7.6
, which will be used in several further proofs.

Lemma 7.5

(Entropy of projection image)
.

Fix a box
W\Subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T,\vartheta\in(0,\infty)
and
S\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there is a projection
\Pi=\Pi_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K,T,\vartheta,S}\colon{\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}\to A_{W;\mathfrak{r},M,L,K;T}\cap({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({M,\vartheta,S}})}}})
and there are
\varepsilon^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{M,W,L}
and
\varepsilon_{M,\vartheta,S,T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\in(0,1)
satisfying, for any
S
,

\lim_{L\to\infty}\lim_{M\to\infty}\limsup_{W\uparrow\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varepsilon_{M,W,L}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}=0\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\lim_{M,\vartheta,T\to\infty}\varepsilon_{M,\vartheta,S,T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}=0,

such that, for any
\xi\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathcal{L}}_{W}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{W})
with finite expected total particle number
\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{W}\rangle
, and for any
S\in\mathbb{N}
,

J_{W}(\xi\circ\Pi^{-1})\leq J_{W}(\xi)+\varepsilon^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}_{M,W,L}+\Big(\frac{1}{K}+\varepsilon_{M,\vartheta,S,T}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}\Big)\frac{1}{|W|}\langle\xi,\mathfrak{N}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({\ell}})}}}_{W}\rangle.

(7.23)

Proof.

For the ease of notation, we write
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}
instead of
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}(\xi)
and
\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
instead of
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
and
\partial\xi^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}
instead of
\partial\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{S}}}})}}}(\xi)
. Furthermore, we write
{\tt Q}_{W}
instead of
\Pi_{W}^{{{\scriptscriptstyle{({{\mathcal{L}}}})}}}({\tt Q})
. Furthermore, we drop the indices
W
,
\mathfrak{r},M,L,K
, and
